\displaystyle=\Big(\frac{n^{2}m^{2}_{1}}{|n-m_{1}|+|n|}-\frac{1}{2}|n|m^{2}_{1}\Big)+\Big(\frac{n|n|m_{1}(|n-m_{1}|-|n|)}{|n-m_{1}|+|n|}+\frac{1}{2}|n|m^{2}_{1}\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{|n|m^{2}_{1}(|n|-|n-m_{1}|)}{2(|n-m_{1}|+|n|)}+\frac{n^{2}m^{2}_{1}(|n-m_{1}|-|n|)+\frac{1}{2}|n|m^{4}_{1}}{(|n-m_{1}|+|n|)^{2}}.

To sum up, the desired estimate (
3.8
) is obtained.

(2)
For any
A\in\mathcal{A}_{0}\setminus\{S_{0}A_{0}S_{0}\}
, denote

\mathcal{K}_{A}(\mu,n,m)=16\mu^{3}\big[R^{+}_{0}(\mu^{4})vAv(R^{+}_{0}-R^{-}_{0})(\mu^{4})\big](n,m),

(3.21)

then it follows from Lemma
3.2
that

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{A}(\mu,n,m)=\left\{\begin{aligned} &\sum\limits_{m_{1},m_{2}}\int_{[0,1]^{2}}\mathcal{M}^{0}_{21}(N_{1},M_{2},m_{1},m_{2})(f_{21}^{+}-f_{21}^{-})(\mu,N_{1},M_{2})d\rho_{1}d\rho_{2},\quad A=\mu^{2}QA^{0}_{21}Q,\\
&\sum\limits_{m_{1},m_{2}}\Big[\int_{0}^{1}\mathcal{M}^{0}_{22}(\rho_{1},m_{1},m_{2})\big(f^{+,1}_{22}+f^{-,1}_{22}\big)(\mu,N_{1},\widetilde{M}_{2})d\rho_{1}+\\
&\qquad\qquad\big(f^{+,2}_{22}+f^{-,2}_{22}\big)(\mu,\widetilde{M}_{2},n,m_{1},m_{2})\Big],\qquad\qquad\quad A=\mu^{2}S_{0}A^{0}_{22},\\
&\sum\limits_{m_{1},m_{2}}\int_{0}^{1}\mathcal{M}^{0}_{23}(\rho_{2},m_{1},m_{2})(f^{+}_{23}+f^{-}_{23})(\mu,\widetilde{N}_{1},M_{2})d\rho_{2},\quad A=\mu^{2}A^{0}_{23}S_{0},\\
&\sum\limits_{m_{1},m_{2}}\int_{0}^{1}\mathcal{M}^{0}_{31}(N_{1},m_{1},m_{2})(f^{+}_{31}+f^{-}_{31})(\mu,{N}_{1},\widetilde{M}_{2})d\rho_{1},\quad A=\mu^{3}QA^{0}_{31},\\
&\sum\limits_{m_{1},m_{2}}\int_{0}^{1}\mathcal{M}^{0}_{32}(M_{2},m_{1},m_{2})(f^{+}_{32}-f^{-}_{32})(\mu,\widetilde{{N}}_{1},{M}_{2})d\rho_{2},\quad A=\mu^{3}A^{0}_{32}Q,\end{aligned}\right.

(3.22)

where
N_{1}=n-\rho_{1}m_{1}
,
\widetilde{N}_{1}=n-m_{1}
,
M_{2}=m-\rho_{2}m_{2}
,
\widetilde{M}_{2}=m-m_{2}
,

\displaystyle\Phi^{\pm}_{1}(\mu,X,Y)

\displaystyle=e^{-i\theta_{+}(|X|\pm|Y|)},\quad\Phi^{\pm}_{2}(\mu,X,Y)=e^{b(\mu)|X|\pm i\theta_{+}|Y|},

\displaystyle f^{\pm}_{21}(\mu,N_{1},M_{2})

\displaystyle=\mu^{-1}\theta^{2}_{+}a_{11}(\mu)\Phi^{\pm}_{1}(\mu,N_{1},M_{2})-\mu^{-1}\theta_{+}b(\mu)a_{12}(\mu)\Phi^{\pm}_{2}(\mu,N_{1},M_{2}),

\displaystyle f^{\pm,1}_{22}(\mu,N_{1},\widetilde{M}_{2})

\displaystyle=\mu^{-1}\theta^{2}_{+}a_{11}(\mu)\Phi^{\pm}_{1}(\mu,N_{1},\widetilde{M}_{2})+i\mu^{-1}(b(\mu))^{2}a_{12}(\mu)\Phi^{\pm}_{2}(\mu,N_{1},\widetilde{M}_{2}),

\displaystyle f^{\pm,2}_{22}(\mu,\widetilde{M}_{2},n,m_{1},m_{2})

\displaystyle=i\mu^{-1}a_{1}(\mu)c_{3}(\mu)e^{\pm i\theta_{+}|\widetilde{M}_{2}|}|n-m_{1}|v(m_{1})(S_{0}A^{0}_{22}v)(m_{1},m_{2}),

\displaystyle f^{\pm}_{23}(\mu,\widetilde{N}_{1},M_{2})

\displaystyle=\mu^{-1}\theta^{2}_{+}a_{11}(\mu)\Phi^{\pm}_{1}(\mu,\widetilde{N}_{1},M_{2})-i\mu^{-1}\theta^{2}_{+}a_{12}(\mu)\Phi^{\pm}_{2}(\mu,\widetilde{N}_{1},M_{2}),

\displaystyle f^{\pm}_{31}(\mu,N_{1},\widetilde{M}_{2})

\displaystyle=-i\theta_{+}a_{11}(\mu)\Phi^{\pm}_{1}(\mu,{N}_{1},\widetilde{M}_{2})-ib(\mu)a_{12}(\mu)\Phi^{\pm}_{2}(\mu,{N}_{1},\widetilde{M}_{2}),

\displaystyle f^{\pm}_{32}(\mu,\widetilde{N}_{1},M_{2})

\displaystyle=-i{\theta_{+}}a_{11}(\mu)\Phi^{\pm}_{1}(\mu,\widetilde{{N}}_{1},{M}_{2})+{\theta_{+}}a_{12}(\mu)\Phi^{\pm}_{2}(\mu,\widetilde{{N}}_{1},{M}_{2}),

(3.23)

with
a_{11}(\mu)=(a_{1}(\mu))^{2}
,

a_{12}(\mu)=a_{1}(\mu)a_{2}(\mu)
, and

•

\mathcal{M}^{0}_{21}(N_{1},M_{2},m_{1},m_{2})=({\rm sign}(N_{1}))({\rm sign}(M_{2}))(v_{1}QA^{0}_{21}Qv_{1})(m_{1},m_{2})
,

•

\mathcal{M}^{0}_{22}(\rho_{1},m_{1},m_{2})=(1-\rho_{1})(v_{2}S_{0}A^{0}_{22}v)(m_{1},m_{2})
,

•

\mathcal{M}^{0}_{23}(\rho_{2},m_{1},m_{2})=(1-\rho_{2})(vA^{0}_{23}S_{0}v_{2})(m_{1},m_{2})
,

•

\mathcal{M}^{0}_{31}(N_{1},m_{1},m_{2})=({\rm sign}(N_{1}))(v_{1}QA^{0}_{31}v)(m_{1},m_{2})
,

•

\mathcal{M}^{0}_{32}(M_{2},m_{1},m_{2})=({\rm sign}(M_{2}))(vA^{0}_{32}Qv_{1})(m_{1},m_{2})
.

From (
3.23
), we note that for any operator
A\in\mathcal{A}_{0}\setminus\{S_{0}A_{0}S_{0}\}
, the estimates of
K_{A}(n,m)
can be reduced to the three fundamental cases presented in (
3.1
), (
3.1
) and (
3.1
). Using analogous arguments to those employed previously, we can obtain

|K_{A}(n,m)|\lesssim\left<|n|\pm|m|\right>^{-2},\quad A\in\mathcal{A}_{0}\setminus\{S_{0}A_{0}S_{0}\},

which gives
K_{A}\in\mathbb{B}(\ell^{p}(\mathbb{Z}))
for all
1\leq p\leq\infty
. This completes the whole proof.
∎

Proposition 3.6
.

Under the assumptions in Proposition
3.5
, let
K^{0}_{4}
be the operator with kernel defined in (
3.6
), then
K^{0}_{4}\in\mathbb{B}(\ell^{p}(\mathbb{Z}))
for any
1\leq p\leq\infty
.

Proof.

It follows from (
3.6
) and (
2.4
) that

\displaystyle K^{0}_{4}(n,m)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{2}\mu^{3}\chi_{1}(\mu)\big[R^{+}_{0}(\mu^{4})v\Gamma^{0}_{4}(\mu)v(R^{+}_{0}-R^{-}_{0})(\mu^{4})\big](n,m)d\mu=\frac{1}{16}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{2}(K^{+}_{4j}+K^{-}_{4j})(n,m),

where
N_{1}=n-m_{1}
,
M_{2}=m-m_{2}
,
\widetilde{\Gamma}^{0}_{4}(\mu)=\frac{\Gamma^{0}_{4}(\mu)}{\mu^{4}}
and

\displaystyle K^{\pm}_{41}(n,m)

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{2}e^{-i\theta_{+}(|n|\pm|m|)}\mu\underbrace{\chi_{1}(\mu)a_{11}(\mu)}_{\widetilde{\chi}_{1}(\mu)}\sum\limits_{m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-i\theta_{+}\big(|N_{1}|-|n|\pm(|M_{2}|-|m|)\big)}

\displaystyle\quad\times(v\widetilde{\Gamma}^{0}_{4}(\mu)v)(m_{1},m_{2})d\mu:=\int_{0}^{2}e^{-i\theta_{+}(|n|\pm|m|)}\mu\widetilde{\chi}_{1}(\mu)L^{\pm}_{41}(\mu,n,m)d\mu,

(3.24)

\displaystyle K^{\pm}_{42}(n,m)

\displaystyle=i\int_{0}^{2}e^{b(\mu)|n|\pm i\theta_{+}|m|}\mu\underbrace{\chi_{1}(\mu)a_{12}(\mu)}_{\widetilde{\widetilde{\chi}}_{1}(\mu)}\sum\limits_{m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{b(\mu)(|N_{1}|-|n|)\pm i\theta_{+}(|M_{2}|-|m|)}

\displaystyle\quad\times(v\widetilde{\Gamma}^{0}_{4}(\mu)v)(m_{1},m_{2})d\mu:=\int_{0}^{2}e^{b(\mu)|n|\pm i\theta_{+}|m|}\mu\widetilde{\widetilde{\chi}}_{1}(\mu)L^{\pm}_{42}(\mu,n,m)d\mu.

(3.25)

We consider the following homogeneous dyadic partition of unity
\{{\varphi_{N}}\}_{N\in\mathbb{Z}}
on
(0,\infty)
:
\varphi\in C^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{+})
,
0\leq\varphi\leq 1
,
supp~\varphi\subset[\frac{1}{4},1],\ \varphi_{N}(\mu)=\varphi(2^{-N}\mu)
,

supp~\varphi_{N}\subset\left[2^{N-2},2^{N}\right]
,

\sum_{N\in\mathbb{Z}}{}\varphi_{N}(\mu)=1,\quad\mu>0.

Let
N_{0}=[log_{2}(4\mu_{0})]
, then
\widetilde{\chi}_{1}(\mu)
and
\widetilde{\widetilde{\chi}}_{1}(\mu)
can be decomposed as follows:

\displaystyle\widetilde{\chi}_{1}(\mu)=\sum\limits_{N\in\mathbb{Z}}\widetilde{\chi}_{1}(\mu)\varphi_{N}(\mu)=\sum\limits_{N=-\infty}^{N_{0}}\widetilde{\chi}_{1}(\mu)\varphi_{N}(\mu):=\sum\limits_{N=-\infty}^{N_{0}}\tilde{\phi}_{N}(\mu),

(3.26)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\widetilde{\chi}}_{1}(\mu)=\sum\limits_{N\in\mathbb{Z}}\widetilde{\widetilde{\chi}}_{1}(\mu)\varphi_{N}(\mu)=\sum\limits_{N=-\infty}^{N_{0}}\widetilde{\widetilde{\chi}}_{1}(\mu)\varphi_{N}(\mu):=\sum\limits_{N=-\infty}^{N_{0}}\tilde{\tilde{\phi}}_{N}(\mu).

(3.27)

It immediately concludes that for any
s\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\big|\big(\tilde{\phi}_{N}\big)^{(s)}(\mu)\big|+\Big|\Big(\tilde{\tilde{\phi}}_{N}\Big)^{(s)}(\mu)\Big|\leq c(s)2^{-Ns},

(3.28)

where
c(s)
is a constant depending on
s
. Taking (
3.26
) into (
3.24
) and (
3.27
) into (
3.25
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}K^{\pm}_{41}(n,m)&=\sum\limits_{N=-\infty}^{N_{0}}\int_{0}^{2}e^{-i\theta_{+}(|n|\pm|m|)}\mu\tilde{\phi}_{N}(\mu)L^{\pm}_{41}(\mu,n,m)d\mu:=\sum\limits_{N=-\infty}^{N_{0}}K^{\pm,N}_{41}(n,m),\\
K^{\pm}_{42}(n,m)&=\sum\limits_{N=-\infty}^{N_{0}}\int_{0}^{2}e^{b(\mu)|n|\pm i\theta_{+}|m|}\mu\tilde{\tilde{\phi}}_{N}(\mu)L^{\pm}_{42}(\mu,n,m)d\mu:=\sum\limits_{N=-\infty}^{N_{0}}K^{\pm,N}_{42}(n,m).\end{split}

(3.29)

Next we show that for any
N\leq N_{0}
, the following estimates hold:

\displaystyle|K^{\pm,N}_{4j}(n,m)|\lesssim\min\{2^{2N},\left<|n|\pm|m|\right>^{-2}\},\quad\forall\ n,m\in\mathbb{Z},\quad j=1,2,

(3.30)

from which

\displaystyle|K^{\pm,N}_{4j}(n,m)|\lesssim 2^{2N(1-t)}\left<|n|\pm|m|\right>^{-2t},\quad t\in[0,1],\quad\forall\ n,m\in\mathbb{Z},\quad j=1,2.

By choosing
t=\frac{3}{4}
, we obtain

\displaystyle|K^{\pm}_{4j}(n,m)|\leq\sum\limits_{N=-\infty}^{N_{0}}|K^{\pm,N}_{4j}(n,m)|\lesssim\left<|n|\pm|m|\right>^{-\frac{3}{2}}\sum\limits_{N=-\infty}^{N_{0}}2^{\frac{N}{2}}\lesssim\left<|n|\pm|m|\right>^{-\frac{3}{2}},\quad j=1,2,

which together with Lemma
3.4
gives the desired result. To derive (
3.30
), we first note that for any
k=0,1,2
,

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{\mu\in(0,\mu_{0}]}\mu^{k}\Big(\big|(\partial^{k}_{\mu}L^{\pm}_{41})(\mu,n,m)\big|+\big|e^{b(\mu)|n|}(\partial^{k}_{\mu}L^{\pm}_{42})(\mu,n,m)\big|\Big)\lesssim\|\left<\cdot\right>^{2k}V(\cdot)\|_{\ell^{1}},

(3.31)

uniformly in
n,m\in\mathbb{Z}
.

On one hand, combining (
3.29
), the support of
\varphi_{N}
and (
3.31
), one has

\displaystyle|K^{\pm,N}_{4j}(n,m)|\lesssim\int_{supp\varphi_{N}}\mu d\mu=\int_{2^{N-2}}^{2^{N}}\mu d\mu\lesssim 2^{2N}\lesssim 2^{2N_{0}}\lesssim 1,\quad j=1,2.

(3.32)

On the other hand, for any
N\leq N_{0}
, denote

G^{\pm,N}_{41}(\mu,n,m)=\mu\tilde{\phi}_{N}(\mu)L^{\pm}_{41}(\mu,n,m).

Assuming that
||n|\pm|m||\geq 1
and applying integration by parts twice to
K^{\pm,N}_{41}(n,m)
, we obtain

\displaystyle K^{\pm,N}_{41}(n,m)=\Big(\frac{e^{-i\theta_{+}(|n|\pm|m|)}}{-i\theta^{\prime}_{+}(\mu)(|n|\pm|m|)}G^{\pm,N}_{41}(\mu,n,m)\Big)\Big|^{2}_{0}-\int_{0}^{2}\frac{e^{-i\theta_{+}(|n|\pm|m|)}}{-i(|n|\pm|m|)}\big(\widetilde{G}^{\pm,N}_{41}\big)^{\prime}(\mu,n,m)d\mu

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{2}\frac{e^{-i\theta_{+}(|n|\pm|m|)}}{i(|n|\pm|m|)}\big(\widetilde{G}^{\pm,N}_{41}\big)^{\prime}(\mu,n,m)d\mu

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{(|n|\pm|m|)^{2}}\int_{0}^{2}e^{-i\theta_{+}(|n|\pm|m|)}\Big(g(\mu)\big(\widetilde{G}^{\pm,N}_{41}\big)^{\prime}(\mu,n,m)\Big)^{\prime}d\mu=O((|n|\pm|m|)^{-2}),

where

\theta^{\prime}_{+}(\mu)=-(1-\frac{\mu^{2}}{4})^{\frac{-1}{2}},\quad\widetilde{G}^{\pm,N}_{41}(\mu,n,m)=(\theta^{\prime}_{+}(\mu))^{-1}G^{\pm,N}_{41}(\mu,n,m):=g(\mu)G^{\pm,N}_{41}(\mu,n,m),

and in both second and third equalities we used the facts that
supp\chi_{1}\subseteq[0,\mu_{0}]
and
\varphi(0)=0
. For the fourth equality, first we can compute that

\displaystyle\Big(g(\mu)\big(\widetilde{G}^{\pm,N}_{41}\big)^{\prime}(\mu,n,m)\Big)^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\Big[\Big(g(\mu)g^{(2)}(\mu)+(g^{\prime}(\mu))^{2}\Big)G^{\pm,N}_{41}(\mu,n,m)+3g^{\prime}(\mu)g(\mu)\big(G^{\pm,N}_{41}\big)^{\prime}(\mu,n,m)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+(g(\mu))^{2}\big(G^{\pm,N}_{41}\big)^{(2)}(\mu,n,m)\Big].

For any
\mu\in(0,\mu_{0}]\cap[2^{N-2},2^{N}]
, it follows from (
3.31
) and (
3.28
) that

\displaystyle\big|(\partial^{k}_{\mu}G^{\pm,N}_{41})(\mu,n,m)\big|\lesssim 2^{N(1-k)},\quad k=0,1,2,\quad{\rm uniformly\ in}\ n,m\in\mathbb{Z},

which together with the smoothness of
g(\mu)
on
(0,\mu_{0}]
gives the desired fourth equality. Through an analogous argument and using the properties of
\alpha^{\pm}(\mu,n,m)
defined in (
3.17
), we can verify that the same bound also holds for
K^{\pm,N}_{42}(n,m)
. Therefore, the desired (
3.30
) is obtained.∎

Next, we turn to establish the boundedness of operators in the class
O(1)
. For such integral operators, we shall need the following key lemma.

Lemma 3.7
.

Let
\phi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R},\mathbb{R})
be such that
\phi(s)=0
for
0\leq s\leq 1
and
\phi(s)=1
for
s\geq 2
. Define
k^{\pm}_{j}
be the integral operator with the following kernel
k^{\pm}_{j}(n,m)
:

k^{\pm}_{1}(n,m)=\frac{\phi(||n|\pm|m||^{2})}{|n|\pm|m|},\quad k^{\pm}_{2}(n,m)=\frac{\phi(||n|-|m||^{2})}{|n|\pm i|m|}

then
k^{\pm}_{1},k^{\pm}_{2}\in\mathbb{B}(\ell^{p}(\mathbb{Z}))
for
1<p<\infty
.

The proof of this lemma will be postponed at the end of this section.

Proposition 3.8
.

Under the assumptions in Proposition
3.5
, let
K_{1}
and
K_{P_{1}}
be the operators with kernels defined in (
3.4
) and (
3.5
), respectively. Then
K_{1},K_{P_{1}}\in\mathbb{B}(\ell^{p}(\mathbb{Z}))
for all
1<p<\infty
.

Proof.

(1) For
{\bm{K_{P_{1}}}}
, it follows from (
3.5
) and (
2.4
) that

\displaystyle K_{P_{1}}(n,m)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{16}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{2}\int_{0}^{2}ia_{1}(\mu)\chi_{1}(\mu)\sum\limits_{m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}({I}^{+}_{j}+{I}^{-}_{j})(\mu,N_{1},M_{2})(vP_{1}v)(m_{1},m_{2})d\mu,

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{16}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{2}(K^{+,j}_{P_{1}}+K^{-,j}_{P_{1}})(n,m),

(3.33)

where
N_{1}=n-m_{1}
,
M_{2}=m-m_{2}
and

{I}^{\pm}_{1}(\mu,N_{1},M_{2})=ia_{1}(\mu)e^{-i\theta_{+}(|N_{1}|\pm|M_{2}|)},\quad{I}^{\pm}_{2}(\mu,N_{1},M_{2})=a_{2}(\mu)e^{b(\mu)|N_{1}|\pm i\theta_{+}|M_{2}|}.

By virtue of (
3.9
), (
3.10
) and (
3.14
),
K^{\pm,j}_{P_{1}}(n,m)
can be written as follows, respectively:

\displaystyle K^{\pm,1}_{P_{1}}(n,m)

\displaystyle=\int_{-\pi}^{0}e^{-i\theta_{+}(|n|\pm|m|)}\tilde{\chi}_{1}(\mu(\theta_{+}))L^{\pm,1}_{P_{1}}(\theta_{+},n,m)d\theta_{+}:=\int_{-\pi}^{0}e^{-i\theta_{+}(|n|\pm|m|)}G^{\pm,1}_{P_{1}}(\theta_{+},n,m)d\theta_{+},

\displaystyle K^{\pm,2}_{P_{1}}(n,m)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{2}e^{b(\mu)|n|\pm i\theta_{+}|m|}\tilde{\tilde{\chi}}_{1}(\mu)L^{\pm,2}_{P_{1}}(\mu,n,m)d\mu:=\int_{0}^{2}e^{b(\mu)|n|\pm i\theta_{+}|m|}G^{\pm,2}_{P_{1}}(\mu,n,m)d\mu,

where
\tilde{\chi}_{1}(\mu)=-(1-\frac{\mu^{2}}{4})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\chi_{1}(\mu)
,
\tilde{\tilde{\chi}}_{1}(\mu)=-i(1-\frac{\mu^{4}}{16})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\chi_{1}(\mu)
and

\displaystyle L^{\pm,1}_{P_{1}}(\theta_{+},n,m)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-i\theta_{+}\big(|N_{1}|-|n|\pm(|M_{2}|-|m|)\big)}(vP_{1}v)(m_{1},m_{2}),

\displaystyle L^{\pm,2}_{P_{1}}(\mu,n,m)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{b(\mu)(|N_{1}|-|n|)\pm i\theta_{+}(|M_{2}|-|m|)}(vP_{1}v)(m_{1},m_{2}).

Similarly, for any
k=0,1,2
, we can establish the following estimates:

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{\theta_{+}\in(-\pi,0)}\big|(\partial^{k}_{\theta_{+}}L^{\pm,1}_{P_{1}})(\theta_{+},n,m)\big|+\sup_{\mu\in(0,\mu_{0}]}\big|e^{b(\mu)|n|}(\partial^{k}_{\mu}L^{\pm,2}_{P_{1}})(\mu,n,m)\big|\lesssim\|\left<\cdot\right>^{2k}V(\cdot)\|_{\ell^{1}},

uniformly in
n,m\in\mathbb{Z}
. This immediately yields the uniform boundedness of
K^{\pm,j}_{P_{1}}(n,m)
on
\mathbb{Z}^{2}
for
j=1,2
. We consider decomposing
K^{\pm,j}_{P_{1}}(n,m)
as follows:

K^{\pm,j}_{P_{1}}(n,m)=\left\{\begin{aligned} &\phi_{\pm}K^{\pm,1}_{P_{1}}(n,m)+(1-\phi_{\pm})K^{\pm,1}_{P_{1}}(n,m),&if\ j=1,\\
&\phi_{-}K^{\pm,2}_{P_{1}}(n,m)+(1-\phi_{-})K^{\pm,2}_{P_{1}}(n,m),&if\ j=2,\end{aligned}\right.

(3.34)

where
\phi_{\pm}:=\phi\left(||n|\pm|m||^{2}\right)
with
\phi
as defined in Lemma
3.7
. For the second terms in (
3.34
), the boundedness of
K^{\pm,j}_{P_{1}}(n,m)
combined with the support of
\phi_{\pm}
implies

\displaystyle(1-\phi_{\pm})K^{\pm,1}_{P_{1}}(n,m)=O(\left<|n|\pm|m|\right>^{-2}),\quad(1-\phi_{-})K^{\pm,2}_{P_{1}}(n,m)=O(\left<|n|\pm|m|\right>^{-2}).

(3.35)

For the first terms, using the method for
K^{\pm,1}_{0}
and
K^{\pm,2}_{0}
in Proposition
3.5
, respectively, we obtain

\displaystyle\phi_{\pm}K^{\pm,1}_{P_{1}}(n,m)

\displaystyle=\phi_{\pm}\lim\limits_{\theta_{+}\rightarrow 0}\frac{ie^{-i\theta_{+}(|n|\pm|m|)}}{|n|\pm|m|}G^{\pm,1}_{P_{1}}(\theta_{+},n,m)+\int_{-\pi}^{0}\frac{\phi_{\pm}e^{-i\theta_{+}(|n|\pm|m|)}}{i(|n|\pm|m|)}\frac{\partial{G^{\pm,1}_{P_{1}}}}{\partial\theta_{+}}(\theta_{+},n,m)d\theta_{+}

\displaystyle=2(i-1)k^{\pm}_{1}(n,m)+O(\left<|n|\pm|m|\right>^{-2}),

(3.36)

\displaystyle\phi_{-}K^{\pm,2}_{P_{1}}(n,m)

\displaystyle=-\lim\limits_{\mu\rightarrow 0}\frac{\phi_{-}e^{b(\mu)|n|\pm i\theta_{+}|m|}}{\alpha^{\pm}(\mu,n,m)}G^{\pm,2}_{P_{1}}(\mu,n,m)-\int_{0}^{2}\phi_{-}e^{b(\mu)|n|\pm i\theta_{+}|m|}\Big(\frac{G^{\pm,2}_{P_{1}}}{\alpha^{\pm}}\Big)^{\prime}(\mu,n,m)d\mu,

\displaystyle=2(i-1)k^{\pm}_{2}(n,m)+O(\left<|n|\pm|m|\right>^{-2}),

(3.37)

where
\alpha^{\pm}(\mu,n,m)
and
k^{\pm}_{\ell}(n,m)
are defined in (
3.17
) and Lemma
3.7
, respectively, and we used

\sum\limits_{m_{1},m_{2}}(vP_{1}v)(m_{1},m_{2})=-2(i+1).

Therefore, combining (
3.34
)
\sim
(
3.37
) and (
3.1
), we derive

\displaystyle K_{P_{1}}(n,m)=\frac{i-1}{8}\Big(k^{+}_{1}(n,m)+k^{-}_{1}(n,m)+k^{+}_{2}(n,m)+k^{-}_{2}(n,m)\Big)+O(\left<|n|\pm|m|\right>^{-2}),

(3.38)

which together with Lemmas
3.4
and
3.7
gives that
K_{P_{1}}\in\mathbb{B}(\ell^{p}(\mathbb{Z}))
for all
1<p<\infty
.

(2) For
{\bm{K_{1}}}
, by (
3.4
) and Lemma
3.2
, we have

\displaystyle K_{1}(n,m)=\frac{1}{16}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{2}(K^{+,j}_{1}-K^{-,j}_{1})(n,m),

(3.39)

where
N_{1}=n-\rho_{1}m_{1}
,
M_{2}=m-\rho_{2}m_{2}
and

\displaystyle K^{\pm,1}_{1}(n,m)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{0}^{1}({\rm sign}(N_{1}))\int_{0}^{2}\chi_{1}(\mu)\left(\frac{\theta_{+}}{{\rm sin}\theta_{+}}\right)^{2}\sum\limits_{m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{0}^{1}({\rm sign}(M_{2}))e^{-i\theta_{+}(|N_{1}|\pm|M_{2}|)}d\rho_{2}

\displaystyle\quad\times(v_{1}QA_{1}Qv_{1})(m_{1},m_{2})d\mu d\rho_{1}:=\sum\limits_{m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{0}^{1}({\rm sign}(N_{1}))K^{\pm,1}_{\rho_{1},m_{1}}(n,m)d\rho_{1},

(3.40)

\displaystyle K^{\pm,2}_{1}(n,m)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{0}^{1}({\rm sign}(N_{1}))\int_{0}^{2}{\chi}_{1}(\mu)\frac{\theta_{+}}{{\rm sin}\theta_{+}}\frac{b(\mu)}{\mu}a_{2}(\mu)\sum\limits_{m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{0}^{1}({\rm sign}(M_{2}))e^{b(\mu)|N_{1}|\pm i\theta_{+}|M_{2}|}d\rho_{2}

\displaystyle\quad\times(v_{1}QA_{1}Qv_{1})(m_{1},m_{2})d\mu d\rho_{1}:=\sum\limits_{m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{0}^{1}({\rm sign}(N_{1}))K^{\pm,2}_{\rho_{1},m_{1}}(n,m)d\rho_{1}.

For any fixed parameters
(\rho_{1},m_{1})\in[0,1]\times\mathbb{Z}
, we shall establish the following estimates:

\displaystyle\begin{split}K^{\pm,1}_{\rho_{1},m_{1}}(n,m)&=ik^{\pm}_{1}(n,m)C_{1}(m_{1},m)+O\Big(\mathcal{M}_{1}(m_{1})\left<|n|\pm|m|\right>^{-2}\Big),\\
K^{\pm,2}_{\rho_{1},m_{1}}(n,m)&=k^{\pm}_{2}(n,m)C_{1}(m_{1},m)+O\Big(\mathcal{M}_{1}(m_{1})\left<|n|\pm|m|\right>^{-2}\Big),\end{split}

(3.41)

where
\mathcal{M}_{1}(m_{1})=\left<m_{1}\right>^{3}|v(m_{1})|\big(|QA_{1}Q|(\left<\cdot\right>^{3}|v(\cdot)|)\big)(m_{1})
and

\displaystyle C_{1}(m_{1},m)=\sum\limits_{m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}(v_{1}QA_{1}Qv_{1})(m_{1},m_{2})\int_{0}^{1}({\rm sign}(M_{2}))d\rho_{2}.

Once this is established, noting that
|C_{1}(m_{1},m)|\leq\mathcal{M}_{1}(m_{1})
uniformly in
m\in\mathbb{Z}
, which combined with Lemmas
3.4
and
3.7
and triangle inequality, yields that
K^{\pm,j}_{\rho_{1},m_{1}}
is
\ell^{p}
bounded for any
1<p<\infty
and satisfies

\displaystyle\|K^{\pm,j}_{\rho_{1},m_{1}}\|_{\ell^{p}\rightarrow\ell^{p}}\lesssim\mathcal{M}_{1}(m_{1}),\quad j=1,2.

(3.42)

Then for any
j=1,2
and
1<p<\infty
, we have

\displaystyle\|K^{\pm,j}_{1}f\|^{p}_{\ell^{p}}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big|\sum\limits_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum\limits_{m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{0}^{1}({\rm sign}(N_{1}))K^{\pm,j}_{\rho_{1},m_{1}}(n,m)d\rho_{1}f(m)\Big|^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\sum\limits_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big(\sum\limits_{m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{0}^{1}|(K^{\pm,j}_{\rho_{1},m_{1}}f)(n)|d\rho_{1}\Big)^{p},

which together with the Minkowski’s inequality and (
3.42
) concludes that

\displaystyle\|K^{\pm,j}_{1}f\|_{\ell^{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum\limits_{m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{0}^{1}\|K^{\pm,j}_{\rho_{1},m_{1}}f\|_{\ell^{p}}d\rho_{1}\leq\|f\|_{\ell^{p}}\sum\limits_{m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{0}^{1}\|K^{\pm,j}_{\rho_{1},m_{1}}\|_{\ell^{p}\rightarrow\ell^{p}}d\rho_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|f\|_{\ell^{p}}\sum\limits_{m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}}\left<m_{1}\right>^{3}|v(m_{1})|\big(|QA_{1}Q|(\left<\cdot\right>^{3}|v(\cdot)|)\big)(m_{1})\lesssim\|f\|_{\ell^{p}}.

This result combined with (
3.39
) establishes that
K_{1}\in\mathbb{B}(\ell^{p}(\mathbb{Z}))
for
1<p<\infty
.

To obtain (
3.41
), it follows from (
3.40
) that for any given
(\rho_{1},m_{1})\in[0,1]\times\mathbb{Z}
,

\displaystyle K^{\pm,1}_{\rho_{1},m_{1}}(n,m)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{2}e^{-i\theta_{+}(|n|\pm|m|)}\chi_{1}(\mu)\left(\frac{\theta_{+}}{{\rm sin}\theta_{+}}\right)^{2}L^{\pm,1}_{\rho_{1},m_{1}}(\theta_{+},n,m)d\mu

\displaystyle{\mathrel{\to}^{{\rm by}\ \eqref{varible substi1}}}\int_{-\pi}^{0}e^{-i\theta_{+}(|n|\pm|m|)}\bar{\chi}_{1}(\mu(\theta_{+}))\frac{\theta^{2}_{+}}{2(1-{\rm cos}\theta_{+})}L^{\pm,1}_{\rho_{1},m_{1}}(\theta_{+},n,m)d\theta_{+}

\displaystyle:=\int_{-\pi}^{0}e^{-i\theta_{+}(|n|\pm|m|)}G^{\pm,1}_{\rho_{1},m_{1}}(\theta_{+},n,m)d\theta_{+},

(3.43)

and

\displaystyle K^{\pm,2}_{\rho_{1},m_{1}}(n,m)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{2}e^{b(\mu)|n|\pm i\theta_{+}|m|}\bar{\bar{\chi}}_{1}(\mu)\frac{\theta_{+}}{{\rm sin}\theta_{+}}\frac{b(\mu)}{\mu}L^{\pm,2}_{\rho_{1},m_{1}}(\mu,n,m)d\mu

\displaystyle:=\int_{0}^{2}e^{b(\mu)|n|\pm i\theta_{+}|m|}G^{\pm,2}_{\rho_{1},m_{1}}(\mu,n,m)d\mu,

(3.44)

where
\bar{\chi}_{1}(\mu)=\chi_{1}(\mu)(1-\frac{\mu^{2}}{4})^{-\frac{1}{2}}
,
\bar{\bar{\chi}}_{1}(\mu)=-\chi_{1}(\mu)(1+\frac{\mu^{2}}{4})^{-\frac{1}{2}}
and

\displaystyle L^{\pm,1}_{\rho_{1},m_{1}}(\theta_{+},n,m)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{0}^{1}({\rm sign}(M_{2}))e^{-i\theta_{+}\big(|N_{1}|-|n|\pm(|M_{2}|-|m|)\big)}d\rho_{2}(v_{1}QA_{1}Qv_{1})(m_{1},m_{2}),

\displaystyle L^{\pm,2}_{\rho_{1},m_{1}}(\mu,n,m)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{0}^{1}({\rm sign}(M_{2}))e^{b(\mu)(|N_{1}|-|n|)\pm i\theta_{+}(|M_{2}|-|m|)}d\rho_{2}(v_{1}QA_{1}Qv_{1})(m_{1},m_{2}).

Then we have the following estimates for any
k=0,1,2
:

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{\theta_{+}\in(-\pi,0)}\big|(\partial^{k}_{\theta_{+}}L^{\pm,1}_{\rho_{1},m_{1}})(\theta_{+},n,m)\big|+\sup_{\mu\in(0,\mu_{0}]}\big|e^{b(\mu)|n|}(\partial^{k}_{\mu}L^{\pm,2}_{\rho_{1},m_{1}})(\mu,n,m)\big|\lesssim\mathcal{M}_{1}(m_{1}),

(3.45)

uniformly in
n,m\in\mathbb{Z}
and
\rho_{1}\in[0,1]
. Moreover, noting that

\lim\limits_{\theta_{+}\rightarrow 0}\Big(\frac{\theta^{2}_{+}}{2(1-{\rm cos}\theta_{+})}\Big)^{(k)},\ \lim\limits_{\mu\rightarrow 0^{+}}\Big(\frac{b(\mu)}{\mu}\Big)^{(k)}\ {\rm and}\ \lim\limits_{\mu\rightarrow 0^{+}}\Big(\frac{\theta_{+}}{{\rm sin}\theta_{+}}\Big)^{(k)}\ {\rm exist\ for}\ k=0,1,2.

Following an analogous argument to that used for the operators
K^{\pm,j}_{P_{1}}
, we then obtain the claimed estimates in (
3.41
). This completes the entire proof.
∎

Hence, combining Propositions
3.5
,
3.6
and
3.8
and (
3.3
), Theorem
3.1
holds for the regular case.

3.2.
0
is a first kind resonance of
H

In this subsection, we consider the case where
0
is a first kind resonance of
H
. As before, taking the expansion (
2.9
)

\displaystyle\begin{split}M^{-1}(\mu)&=\mu^{-1}S_{1}A_{-1}S_{1}+\big(S_{0}A^{1}_{01}Q+QA^{1}_{02}S_{0}\big)+\mu\big(S_{0}A^{1}_{11}+A^{1}_{12}S_{0}+QA^{1}_{13}Q\big)\\
&\quad+\mu^{2}\big(QA^{1}_{21}+A^{1}_{22}Q\big)+\mu^{3}(QA^{1}_{31}+A^{1}_{32}Q)+\mu^{3}P_{1}+\mu^{3}A^{1}_{33}+\Gamma^{1}_{4}(\mu)\end{split}

into (
3.2
), we obtain

\mathcal{K}_{1}=\sum\limits_{A\in\mathcal{A}_{11}\cup\mathcal{A}_{12}}K_{A}+K_{P_{1}}+K^{1}_{4},

(3.46)

where
\mathcal{A}_{11}=\{\mu^{-1}S_{1}A_{-1}S_{1},S_{0}A^{1}_{01}Q,QA^{1}_{02}S_{0},\mu S_{0}A^{1}_{11},\mu A^{1}_{12}S_{0},\mu QA^{1}_{13}Q,\mu^{2}QA^{1}_{21},\mu^{2}A^{1}_{22}Q,\mu^{3}A^{1}_{33}\},

\mathcal{A}_{12}=\{\mu^{3}QA^{1}_{31},\mu^{3}A^{1}_{32}Q\}
,
K_{A},K_{P_{1}}
are defined in (
3.5
) and

K^{1}_{4}(n,m)=\int_{0}^{2}\mu^{3}\chi_{1}(\mu)\big[R^{+}_{0}(\mu^{4})v\Gamma^{1}_{4}(\mu)v(R^{+}_{0}-R^{-}_{0})(\mu^{4})\big](n,m)d\mu.

Similarly, based on (
3.1
), we can classify these integral operators into two groups:

\displaystyle O(1):K~(K\in\{K_{A}:A\in\mathcal{A}_{11}\}\cup\{K_{P_{1}}\}),\quad O(\mu):K~(K\in\{K^{1}_{4}\}\cup\{K_{A}:A\in\mathcal{A}_{12}\}).

Recalling the results in Propositions
3.5
,
3.6
and
3.8
, we have derived the following
\ell^{p}
boundedness:

•

K\in\mathbb{B}(\ell^{p}(\mathbb{Z}))\ {\rm for\ all\ }1\leq p\leq\infty
with
K\in\{K^{1}_{4}\}\cup\{K_{A}:A\in\mathcal{A}_{12}\}
,

•

K_{A},K_{P_{1}}\in\mathbb{B}(\ell^{p}(\mathbb{Z}))\ {\rm for\ all\ }1<p<\infty
with
A=\mu QA^{1}_{13}Q
.

Therefore, in this case, it suffices to establish the boundedness for
\{K_{A}:A\in\mathcal{A}_{11}\setminus\{\mu QA^{1}_{13}Q\}\}
.

Proposition 3.9
.

Let
H=\Delta^{2}+V
with
|V(n)|\lesssim\left<n\right>^{-\beta}
for
\beta>19
. Suppose that
H
has no positive eigenvalues in the interval
\rm{(}0,16\rm{)}
and
0
is a first kind resonance of
H
. Let
\mathcal{A}_{11}
be defined in (
3.46
). Then for any
A\in\mathcal{A}_{11}\setminus\{\mu QA^{1}_{13}Q\}
,
K_{A}\in\mathbb{B}(\ell^{p}(\mathbb{Z}))
for all
1<p<\infty
and therefore
\mathcal{K}_{1}\in\mathbb{B}(\ell^{p}(\mathbb{Z}))
for all
1<p<\infty
.

Proof.

(1)
For
A=\mu^{-1}S_{1}A_{-1}S_{1}
, denote

\displaystyle\bm{K_{-1}(n,m)}=\int_{0}^{2}\mu^{2}\chi_{1}(\mu)\big[R^{+}_{0}(\mu^{4})vS_{1}A_{-1}S_{1}v(R^{+}_{0}-R^{-}_{0})(\mu^{4})\big](n,m)d\mu.

Since
S_{1}
has the same cancelation as
S_{0}
, it follows directly from (
3.7
), (
3.1
), (
3.1
), (
3.1
) that

\displaystyle K_{-1}(n,m)=\frac{1}{16}\big(K^{(1)}_{-1}+K^{(2)}_{-1}\big)(n,m),

(3.47)

where
\widetilde{\chi}_{13}(\mu)=-i\chi_{1}(\mu)\frac{c_{3}(\mu)}{\mu^{2}}
and

\displaystyle K^{(1)}_{-1}(n,m)

\displaystyle=\frac{-i}{4}C_{-1}(k^{+}_{1}+k^{-}_{1}-k^{+}_{2}-k^{-}_{2})(n,m)+O(\left<|n|\pm|m|\right>^{-2}),

\displaystyle K^{(2)}_{-1}(n,m)

\displaystyle=\int_{-\pi}^{0}e^{\pm i\theta_{+}(|n|+|m|)}\widetilde{\chi}_{13}(\mu(\theta_{+}))\frac{\theta^{2}_{+}}{2(1-{\rm cos}\theta_{+})}L^{\pm}_{-1}(\theta_{+},n,m)d\theta_{+},

with

C_{-1}=\sum\limits_{m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}(v_{2}S_{1}A_{-1}S_{1}v_{2})(m_{1},m_{2}),

(3.48)

\displaystyle L^{\pm}_{-1}(\theta_{+},n,m)=\sum\limits_{m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{0}^{1}(1-\rho_{2})e^{\pm i\theta_{+}(|M_{2}|-|m|-|n|)}d\rho_{2}\cdot|n-m_{1}|\big(vS_{1}A_{-1}S_{1}v_{2})(m_{1},m_{2}).

From Lemmas
3.4
and
3.7
, it is clear that
K^{(1)}_{-1}\in\mathbb{B}(\ell^{p}(\mathbb{Z}))
for all
1<p<\infty
. As for
K^{(2)}_{-1}
, since

c_{3}(\mu)=-\frac{1}{3}\mu^{3}-\frac{1}{8}\mu^{4}+O(\mu^{5}),\quad\mu\rightarrow 0^{+},

we can apply the similar method for
K^{\pm,3}_{0}(n,m)
in Proposition
3.5
to obtain

|K^{(2)}_{-1}(n,m)|\lesssim\left<|n|+|m|\right>^{-2},\quad{\rm for\ any\ }n,m\in\mathbb{Z}.

Thus,
K^{(2)}_{-1}\in\mathbb{B}(\ell^{p}(\mathbb{Z}))
for any
1\leq p\leq\infty
and we derive that
K_{-1}\in\mathbb{B}(\ell^{p}(\mathbb{Z}))
for all
1<p<\infty
.

(2)
Let
A\in\mathcal{A}_{11}\setminus\{\mu^{-1}S_{1}A_{-1}S_{1},\mu QA^{1}_{13}Q\}
. We first compute the expression of
\mathcal{K}_{A}(\mu,n,m)
in (
3.21
). Combining the definition of
\mathcal{A}_{11}
and (
3.22
), it remains to consider such expression for
A=S_{0}A^{1}_{01}Q,QA^{1}_{02}S_{0},\mu^{3}A^{1}_{33}
. From Lemma
3.2
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{A}(\mu,n,m)=\left\{\begin{aligned} &\sum\limits_{m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big[\int_{[0,1]^{2}}(1-\rho_{1})({\rm sign}(M_{2}))\big(f^{+,1}_{01}-f^{-,1}_{01}\big)(\mu,N_{1},{M}_{2})d\rho_{1}d\rho_{2}\mathcal{M}^{1}_{01}(m_{1},m_{2})+\\
&\int_{0}^{1}({\rm sign}(M_{2}))\big(f^{+,2}_{01}-f^{-,2}_{01}\big)(\mu,\widetilde{N}_{1},{M}_{2})d\rho_{2}(vS_{0}A^{1}_{01}Qv_{1})(m_{1},m_{2})\Big],\ \ A=S_{0}A^{1}_{01}Q,\\
&\sum\limits_{m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{[0,1]^{2}}(1-\rho_{2})({\rm sign}(N_{1}))(f_{02}^{+}+f_{02}^{-})(\mu,N_{1},M_{2})d\rho_{1}d\rho_{2}\\
&\quad\times\mathcal{M}^{1}_{02}(m_{1},m_{2}),\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad A=QA^{1}_{02}S_{0},\\
&\sum\limits_{m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}(vA^{1}_{33}v)(m_{1},m_{2})(f^{+}_{33}+f^{-}_{33})(\mu,\widetilde{N}_{1},\widetilde{M}_{2})d\rho_{2},\quad A=\mu^{3}A^{1}_{33},\end{aligned}\right.

where
N_{1}=n-\rho_{1}m_{1}
,
\widetilde{N}_{1}=n-m_{1}
,
M_{2}=m-\rho_{2}m_{2}
,
\widetilde{M}_{2}=m-m_{2}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{1}_{01}(m_{1},m_{2})

\displaystyle=(v_{2}S_{0}A^{1}_{01}Qv_{1})(m_{1},m_{2}),\quad\mathcal{M}^{1}_{02}(m_{1},m_{2})=(v_{1}QA^{1}_{02}S_{0}v_{2})(m_{1},m_{2}),

\displaystyle f^{\pm,1}_{01}(\mu,N_{1},M_{2})

\displaystyle=i\mu^{-3}\theta^{3}_{+}a_{11}(\mu)\Phi^{\pm}_{1}(\mu,N_{1},M_{2})+\mu^{-3}\theta_{+}(b(\mu))^{2}a_{12}(\mu)\Phi^{\pm}_{2}(\mu,N_{1},M_{2}),

\displaystyle f^{\pm,2}_{01}(\mu,\widetilde{N}_{1},M_{2})

\displaystyle=\mu^{-3}\theta_{+}c_{3}(\mu)|\widetilde{N}_{1}|e^{\pm i\theta_{+}|M_{2}|},

\displaystyle f^{\pm}_{02}(\mu,N_{1},M_{2})

\displaystyle=i\mu^{-3}\theta^{3}_{+}a_{11}(\mu)\Phi^{\pm}_{1}(\mu,N_{1},M_{2})+i\mu^{-3}\theta^{2}_{+}b(\mu)a_{12}(\mu)\Phi^{\pm}_{2}(\mu,N_{1},M_{2}),

\displaystyle f^{\pm}_{33}(\mu,\widetilde{N}_{1},\widetilde{M}_{2})

\displaystyle=-a_{11}(\mu)\Phi^{\pm}_{1}(\mu,\widetilde{N}_{1},\widetilde{M}_{2})+ia_{12}(\mu)\Phi^{\pm}_{2}(\mu,\widetilde{N}_{1},\widetilde{M}_{2}),

with
a_{11}(\mu)
,
a_{12}(\mu)
,
\Phi^{\pm}_{j}(\mu,X,Y)
defined in (
3.23
). We notice that all these expressions together with (
3.22
) allow
K_{A}(n,m)
to reduce to the operators types in Proposition
3.8
and
K^{(2)}_{-1}
in Proposition
3.9
. Consequently, using an analogous argument, we can derive

\displaystyle K_{A}(n,m)=\left\{\begin{aligned} &\frac{1}{32}h_{1,1}(n,m)C_{01}(m)+r(n,m):=\bm{K_{01}(n,m)},\quad A=S_{0}A^{1}_{01}Q,\\
&\frac{C_{02}(n)}{32}g_{1,i}(n,m)+r(n,m):=\bm{K_{02}(n,m)},\quad A=QA^{1}_{02}S_{0},\\
&\frac{C_{11}}{32}g_{i,-1}(n,m)+r(n,m):=\bm{K_{11}(n,m)},\quad A=\mu S_{0}A^{1}_{11},\\
&\frac{C_{12}}{32}g_{i,i}(n,m)+r(n,m):=\bm{K_{12}(n,m)},\quad A=\mu A^{1}_{12}S_{0},\\
&\frac{-C_{21}(n)}{16}g_{1,i}(n,m)+r(n,m):=\bm{K_{21}(n,m)},\quad A=\mu^{2}QA^{1}_{21},\\
&\frac{1}{16}h_{-1,1}(n,m)C_{22}(m)+r(n,m):=\bm{K_{22}(n,m)},\quad A=\mu^{2}A^{1}_{22}Q,\\
&\frac{-C_{33}}{16}g_{i,i}(n,m)+r(n,m):=\bm{K_{33}(n,m)},\quad A=\mu^{3}A^{1}_{33},\end{aligned}\right.

(3.49)

where
r(n,m)=O(\left<|n|\pm|m|\right>^{-2})
,

g_{a,b}(n,m)=\big(a(k^{+}_{1}+k^{-}_{1})+b(k^{+}_{2}+k^{-}_{2})\big)(n,m),\ h_{a,b}(n,m)=\big(a(k^{+}_{1}-k^{-}_{1})+b(k^{+}_{2}-k^{-}_{2})\big)(n,m),

(3.50)

and

\displaystyle C_{01}(m)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{0}^{1}({\rm sign}(M_{2}))d\rho_{2}\cdot\mathcal{M}^{1}_{01}(m_{1},m_{2}),\quad C_{11}=\sum\limits_{m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}(v_{2}S_{0}A^{1}_{11}v)(m_{1},m_{2}),

\displaystyle C_{02}(n)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{0}^{1}({\rm sign}(N_{1}))d\rho_{1}\cdot\mathcal{M}^{1}_{02}(m_{1},m_{2}),\quad C_{12}=\sum\limits_{m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}(vA^{1}_{12}S_{0}v_{2})(m_{1},m_{2}),

\displaystyle C_{21}(n)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{0}^{1}({\rm sign}(N_{1}))d\rho_{1}\cdot(v_{1}QA^{1}_{21}v)(m_{1},m_{2}),

(3.51)

\displaystyle C_{22}(m)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{0}^{1}({\rm sign}(M_{2}))d\rho_{2}(vA^{1}_{22}Qv_{1})(m_{1},m_{2}),\quad C_{33}=\sum\limits_{m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}(vA^{1}_{33}v)(m_{1},m_{2}).

Therefore, combining the uniform boundedness of
C_{01}(m),C_{02}(n),C_{21}(n)
and
C_{22}(m)
and Lemmas
3.4
and
3.7
, we obtain
K_{A}\in\mathbb{B}(\ell^{p}(\mathbb{Z}))
for all
1<p<\infty
and any
A\in\mathcal{A}_{11}\setminus\{\mu^{-1}S_{1}A_{-1}S_{1},\mu QA^{1}_{13}Q\}
. This completes the whole proof together with
(1)
.
∎

3.3.
0
is a second kind resonance of
H

In this subsection, we handle the case where
0
is a second kind resonance of
H
. Compared to the previous two cases, this scenario exhibits some subtle behavior in boundedness analysis. First, as before, taking the expansion (
(iii)
)

\displaystyle M^{-1}(\mu)

\displaystyle=\frac{S_{2}A_{-3}S_{2}}{\mu^{3}}+\frac{S_{2}A_{-2,1}S_{0}+S_{0}A_{-2,2}S_{2}}{\mu^{2}}+\frac{S_{2}A_{-1,1}Q+QA_{-1,2}S_{2}+S_{0}A_{-1,3}S_{0}}{\mu}

\displaystyle\quad+\big(S_{2}A^{2}_{01}+A^{2}_{02}S_{2}+QA^{2}_{03}S_{0}+S_{0}A^{2}_{04}Q\big)+\mu\big(S_{0}A^{2}_{11}+A^{2}_{12}S_{0}+QA^{2}_{13}Q\big)

\displaystyle\quad+\mu^{2}\big(QA^{2}_{21}+A^{2}_{22}Q\big)+\mu^{3}(QA^{2}_{31}+A^{2}_{32}Q)+\mu^{3}P_{1}+\mu^{3}A^{2}_{33}+\Gamma^{2}_{4}(\mu)

into (
3.2
), we obtain

\mathcal{K}_{1}=\sum\limits_{A\in\mathcal{A}_{21}\cup\mathcal{A}_{22}}K_{A}+K_{P_{1}}+K^{2}_{4},

(3.52)

where
\mathcal{A}_{22}=\{\mu^{3}QA^{2}_{31},\mu^{3}A^{2}_{32}Q\}
,
\mathcal{A}_{21}=\mathcal{A}^{(1)}_{21}\cup\mathcal{A}^{(2)}_{21}
with

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{(1)}_{21}

\displaystyle=\{\mu^{-3}S_{2}A_{-3}S_{2},\mu^{-2}S_{2}A_{-2,1}S_{0},\mu^{-2}S_{0}A_{-2,2}S_{2},\mu^{-1}S_{2}A_{-1,1}Q,\mu^{-1}QA_{-1,2}S_{2},S_{2}A^{2}_{01},A^{2}_{02}S_{2}\}

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{(2)}_{21}

\displaystyle=\{\mu^{-1}S_{0}A_{-1,3}S_{0},QA^{2}_{03}S_{0},S_{0}A^{2}_{04}Q,\mu S_{0}A^{2}_{11},\mu A^{2}_{12}S_{0},\mu QA^{2}_{13}Q,\mu^{2}QA^{2}_{21},\mu^{2}A^{2}_{22}Q,\mu^{3}A^{2}_{33}\}

and
K_{A},K_{P_{1}}
are defined in (
3.3
) and

K^{2}_{4}(n,m)=\int_{0}^{2}\mu^{3}\chi_{1}(\mu)\big[R^{+}_{0}(\mu^{4})v\Gamma^{2}_{4}(\mu)v(R^{+}_{0}-R^{-}_{0})(\mu^{4})\big](n,m)d\mu.

Similarly, based on (
3.1
), we can classify these integral operators into two groups:

\displaystyle O(1):K~(K\in\{K_{A}:A\in\mathcal{A}_{21}\}\cup\{K_{P_{1}}\}),\quad O(\mu):K~(K\in\{K^{2}_{4}\}\cup\{K_{A}:A\in\mathcal{A}_{22}\}).

Recalling the established results in Proposition
3.9
, we have derived the following
\ell^{p}
boundedness:

•

K\in\mathbb{B}(\ell^{p}(\mathbb{Z}))\ {\rm for\ all\ }1\leq p\leq\infty
with
K\in\{K^{2}_{4}\}\cup\{K_{A}:A\in\mathcal{A}_{22}\}
,

•

K_{A},K_{P_{1}}\in\mathbb{B}(\ell^{p}(\mathbb{Z}))\ {\rm for\ all\ }1<p<\infty
and all
A\in\mathcal{A}^{(2)}_{21}
.

Therefore, it suffices to deal with the
\ell^{p}
boundedness of the operators
\{K_{A}:A\in\mathcal{A}^{(1)}_{21}\}
.

Proposition 3.10
.

Let
H=\Delta^{2}+V
with
|V(n)|\lesssim\left<n\right>^{-\beta}
for
\beta>27
. Suppose that
H
has no positive eigenvalues in the interval
\rm{(}0,16\rm{)}
and
0
is a second kind resonance of
H
. Let
\mathcal{A}^{(1)}_{21}
be defined in (
3.52
). Then for any
A\in\mathcal{A}^{(1)}_{21}
,
K_{A}\in\mathbb{B}(\ell^{p}(\mathbb{Z}))
for all
1<p<\infty
and therefore
\mathcal{K}_{1}\in\mathbb{B}(\ell^{p}(\mathbb{Z}))
for all
1<p<\infty
.

Proof.

(1) For
A=\mu^{-3}S_{2}A_{-3}S_{2}
, denote

\displaystyle\bm{K_{-3}(n,m)}:=\int_{0}^{2}\chi_{1}(\mu)\big[R^{+}_{0}(\mu^{4})vS_{2}A_{-3}S_{2}v(R^{+}_{0}-R^{-}_{0})(\mu^{4})\big](n,m)d\mu.

By Lemma
3.2
, it can further be expressed as

K_{-3}(n,m)=\frac{1}{64}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{3}(K^{+,j}_{-3}-K^{-,j}_{-3})(n,m),

where
N_{1}=n-\rho_{1}m_{1}
,
M_{2}=m-\rho_{2}m_{2}
,
a_{1j}(\mu),\Phi^{\pm}_{j}(\mu,N_{1},M_{2})
are defined in (
3.23
)
and

\displaystyle K^{\pm,1}_{-3}(n,m)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{2}\chi_{1}(\mu)\mu^{-6}\theta^{6}_{+}a_{11}(\mu)\sum\limits_{m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{[0,1]^{2}}(1-\rho_{1})^{2}(1-\rho_{2})^{2}({\rm sign}(N_{1}))({\rm sign}(M_{2}))

\displaystyle\quad\times\Phi^{\pm}_{1}(\mu,N_{1},M_{2})d\rho_{1}d\rho_{2}(v_{3}S_{2}A_{-3}S_{2}v_{3})(m_{1},m_{2})d\mu,

\displaystyle K^{\pm,2}_{-3}(n,m)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{2}\chi_{1}(\mu)\mu^{-6}\theta^{3}_{+}(b(\mu))^{3}a_{12}(\mu)\sum\limits_{m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{[0,1]^{2}}(1-\rho_{1})^{2}(1-\rho_{2})^{2}({\rm sign}(N_{1}))({\rm sign}(M_{2}))

\displaystyle\quad\times\Phi^{\pm}_{2}(\mu,N_{1},M_{2})d\rho_{1}d\rho_{2}(v_{3}S_{2}A_{-3}S_{2}v_{3})(m_{1},m_{2})d\mu,

\displaystyle K^{\pm,3}_{-3}(n,m)

\displaystyle=-2\int_{0}^{2}\chi_{1}(\mu)\mu^{-6}\theta^{3}_{+}c_{3}(\mu)a_{1}(\mu)\sum\limits_{m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{0}^{1}(1-\rho_{2})^{2}({\rm sign}(M_{2}))e^{\pm i\theta_{+}|M_{2}|}d\rho_{2}

\displaystyle\quad\times|n-m_{1}|(vS_{2}A_{-3}S_{2}v_{3})(m_{1},m_{2})d\mu.

For the first two terms, using the method for
K_{1}
in Proposition
3.8
, we can obtain

\displaystyle K^{\pm,j}_{-3}(n,m)=\sum\limits_{m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{0}^{1}(1-\rho_{1})^{2}({\rm sign}(N_{1}))\widetilde{K}^{\pm,j}_{-3}(m_{1},n,m)d\rho_{1},\quad j=1,2,

where

\displaystyle\widetilde{K}^{\pm,j}_{-3}(m_{1},n,m)=i^{j}k^{\pm}_{j}(n,m)C_{-3}(m_{1},m)+O(\mathcal{M}_{-3}(m_{1})\left<|n|\pm|m|\right>^{-2})

with
C_{-3}(m_{1},m)=\sum\limits_{m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{0}^{1}(1-\rho_{2})^{2}({\rm sign}(M_{2}))d\rho_{2}(v_{3}S_{2}A_{-3}S_{2}v_{3})(m_{1},m_{2})
and

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{-3}(m_{1})=\left<m_{1}\right>^{5}|v(m_{1})|\big|S_{2}A_{-3}S_{2}\big|(\left<\cdot\right>^{5}|v(\cdot)|)(m_{1}).

This establishes that
K^{\pm,j}_{-3}
is
\ell^{p}
bounded for all
1<p<\infty
for
j=1,2
.
As for the third term, considering the decomposition

e^{\pm i\theta_{+}|M_{2}|}=e^{\pm i\theta_{+}(|m|\pm i|n|)}e^{\pm i\theta_{+}(|M_{2}|-|m|)}e^{\theta_{+}|n|},

we further have

\displaystyle K^{\pm,3}_{-3}(n,m)

\displaystyle=-2\int_{0}^{2}e^{\pm i\theta_{+}(|m|\pm i|n|)}\chi_{1}(\mu)\mu^{-6}\theta^{3}_{+}c_{3}(\mu)a_{1}(\mu)L^{\pm}_{-3}(\theta_{+},n,m)d\mu,

where

L^{\pm}_{-3}(\theta_{+},n,m)=\sum\limits_{m_{1},m_{2}}e^{\theta_{+}|n|}|n-m_{1}|\int_{0}^{1}(1-\rho_{2})^{2}({\rm sign}(M_{2}))e^{\pm i\theta_{+}(|M_{2}|-|m|)}d\rho_{2}(vS_{2}A_{-3}S_{2}v_{3})(m_{1},m_{2}).

By an analogous argument as
L^{\pm,3}_{0}(\theta_{+},n.m)
in (
3.1
), the following estimates also hold for any
k=0,1,2
:

\sup\limits_{\theta_{+}\in(-\pi,0)}\big|(\partial^{k}_{\theta_{+}}L^{\pm}_{-3})(\theta_{+},n,m)\big|\lesssim 1,\quad{\rm uniformly\ in}\ n,m\in\mathbb{Z}.

Then applying the method for
K_{P_{1}}
, we derive

\displaystyle K^{\pm,3}_{-3}(n,m)

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{3}\sum\limits_{m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}}|n-m_{1}|v(m_{1})k^{\mp}_{2}(n,m)(S_{2}A_{-3}S_{2}\varphi_{m})(m_{1})+O(\left<|n|\pm|m|\right>^{-2})

\displaystyle:=K^{\pm}(n,m)+O(\left<|n|\pm|m|\right>^{-2}),

where

\varphi_{m}(m_{2})=v_{3}(m_{2})\int_{0}^{1}(1-\rho_{2})^{2}({\rm sign}(M_{2}))d\rho_{2}.

Notice that a distinction from the previous two cases lies in the occurrence of the singular term
K^{\pm}(n,m)
. To deal with such term, we first use the orthogonality
\big<S_{2}f,v\big>=0
to rewrite

K^{\pm}(n,m)=\frac{2}{3}\sum\limits_{m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}}\underbrace{(|n-m_{1}|-|n|)v(m_{1})}_{:=\phi(n,m_{1})}k^{\mp}_{2}(n,m)(S_{2}A_{-3}S_{2}\varphi_{m})(m_{1}).

(3.53)

For any
1<p<\infty
and
f\in\ell^{p}(\mathbb{Z})
, by Minkowski’s inequality and Lemma
3.7
, we have

\displaystyle\|K^{\pm}f\|_{\ell^{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|f\|_{\ell^{p}}\sum\limits_{m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}}\|\phi(\cdot,m_{1})\|_{\ell^{\infty}}\|k^{\mp}_{2}\|_{\ell^{p}-\ell^{p}}\Big(\big|S_{2}A_{-3}S_{2}\big|(|v_{3}|)\Big)(m_{1})

\displaystyle\lesssim\|f\|_{\ell^{p}}\sum\limits_{m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}}|v_{1}(m_{1})|\Big(\big|S_{2}A_{-3}S_{2}\big|(|v_{3}|)\Big)(m_{1})

\displaystyle\lesssim\|f\|_{\ell^{p}},

where in the last inequality we used the absolute boundedness of
S_{2}A_{-3}S_{2}
and Hölder’s inequality.
Thus,
K^{\pm,3}_{-3}
is
\ell^{p}
bounded for all
1<p<\infty
and this proves that

K_{-3}\in\mathbb{B}(\ell^{p}(\mathbb{Z}))
for
1<p<\infty
.

(2) For any
A\in\mathcal{A}^{(1)}_{21}\setminus\{\mu^{-3}S_{2}A_{-3}S_{2}\}
, let
\phi(n,m_{1})
be as in (
3.53
), by a similar analysis to
K_{-3}
, we can derive

\displaystyle K_{A}(n,m)=\left\{\begin{aligned} &\frac{C_{-2,1}(n)}{32}g_{-1,i}(n,m)+{\bm{\frac{1}{96}\big<(S_{2}A_{-2,1}S_{0}v_{2})(\cdot),\phi(n,\cdot)\big>g_{0,-i}(n,m)}}+r(n,m)\\
&:=\bm{K_{-2,1}(n,m)},\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\ A=\mu^{-2}S_{2}A_{-2,1}S_{0},\\
&\frac{-1}{32}h_{1,1}(n,m)C_{-2,2}(m)+r(n,m):=\bm{K_{-2,2}(n,m)},\quad A=\mu^{-2}S_{0}A_{-2,2}S_{2},\\
&\frac{1}{32}\sum\limits_{m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{0}^{1}(1-\rho_{1})^{2}({\rm sign}(N_{1}))h_{-i,1}(n,m)C_{-1,1}(m_{1},m)d\rho_{1}+{\bm{\frac{1}{48}h_{0,1}(n,m)}}\\
&{\bm{\times\big<(S_{2}A_{-1,1}Q\widetilde{\varphi}_{m})(\cdot),\phi(n,\cdot)\big>}}+r(n,m):=\bm{K_{-1,1}(n,m)},\quad A=\mu^{-1}S_{2}A_{-1,1}Q,\\
&\frac{-1}{32}\sum\limits_{m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{0}^{1}({\rm sign}(N_{1}))h_{i,1}(n,m)C_{-1,2}(m_{1},m)d\rho_{1}+r(n,m):=\bm{K_{-1,2}(n,m)},\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\ A=\mu^{-1}QA_{-1,2}S_{2},\\
&\frac{C^{(2)}_{01}(n)}{32}g_{1,-i}(n,m)+{\bm{\frac{1}{48}\big<(S_{2}A^{2}_{01}v)(\cdot),\phi(n,\cdot)\big>g_{0,i}(n,m)}}+r(n,m):=\bm{K^{(2)}_{01}(n,m)},\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\ A=S_{2}A^{2}_{01},\\
&\frac{1}{32}h_{1,-1}(n,m)C^{2}_{02}(m)+r(n,m):=\bm{K^{(2)}_{02}(n,m)},\quad A=A^{2}_{02}S_{2},\end{aligned}\right.

(3.54)

where
r(n,m)=O(\left<|n|\pm|m|\right>^{-2})
,
\widetilde{\varphi}_{m}(m_{2})=v_{1}(m_{2})\int_{0}^{1}({\rm sign}(M_{2}))d\rho_{2}
and

\displaystyle C_{-2,1}(n)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum\limits_{m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{0}^{1}(1-\rho_{1})^{2}({\rm sign}(N_{1}))d\rho_{1}(v_{3}S_{2}A_{-2,1}S_{0}v_{2})(m_{1},m_{2}),

\displaystyle C_{-2,2}(m)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum\limits_{m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{0}^{1}(1-\rho_{2})^{2}({\rm sign}(M_{2}))d\rho_{2}(v_{2}S_{0}A_{-2,2}S_{2}v_{3})(m_{1},m_{2}),

\displaystyle C_{-1,1}(m_{1},m)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{0}^{1}({\rm sign}(M_{2}))d\rho_{2}(v_{3}S_{2}A_{-1,1}Qv_{1})(m_{1},m_{2}),

\displaystyle C_{-1,2}(m_{1},m)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{0}^{1}(1-\rho_{2})^{2}({\rm sign}(M_{2}))d\rho_{2}(v_{1}QA_{-1,2}S_{2}v_{3})(m_{1},m_{2}),

\displaystyle C^{(2)}_{01}(n)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{0}^{1}(1-\rho_{1})^{2}({\rm sign}(N_{1}))d\rho_{1}(v_{3}S_{2}A^{2}_{01}v)(m_{1},m_{2}),

\displaystyle C^{2}_{02}(m)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{0}^{1}(1-\rho_{2})^{2}({\rm sign}(M_{2}))d\rho_{2}(vA^{2}_{02}S_{2}v_{3})(m_{1},m_{2}).

Therefore, we prove that
K_{A}\in\mathbb{B}(\ell^{p}(\mathbb{Z}))
for all
1<p<\infty
and any
A\in\mathcal{A}^{(1)}_{21}
and complete the whole proof.
∎

Therefore, combining Subsections
3.1
,
3.2
and
3.3
, Theorem
3.1
is derived. Finally, we end this section with the proof of Lemma
3.7
.

Proof of Lemma
3.7
.

Let
\chi_{\pm}=\chi_{\mathbb{Z}^{\pm}}
be the characteristic function on
\mathbb{Z}^{\pm}:=\{m\in\mathbb{Z}:\pm m>0\}
and define
(\tau f)(n)=f(-n)
. We introduce the kernel functions:

\displaystyle\widetilde{k}_{1}(n,m)=\frac{\phi(|n-m|^{2})}{n-m},\quad\widetilde{k}^{\pm}_{2}(n,m)=\frac{\phi(|n-m|^{2})}{n\pm im}.

The
\ell^{p}
boundedness of the operators
k^{\pm}_{1}
and
k^{\pm}_{2}
can be reduced to that of
\widetilde{k}_{1}
and
\widetilde{k}^{\pm}_{2}
through the following relations:

({k}^{\pm}_{1}f)(n)=\big[(\chi_{+}\widetilde{k}_{1}\chi_{\mp}-\chi_{-}\widetilde{k}_{1}\chi_{\pm})(1+\tau)f\big](n),

(3.55)

\displaystyle({k}^{\pm}_{2}f)(n)=\big[(\chi_{+}\widetilde{k}^{\pm}_{2}\chi_{+}-\chi_{-}\widetilde{k}^{\pm}_{2}\chi_{-})(1+\tau)f\big](n).

(3.56)

Indeed, noting that
k^{\pm}_{1}(n,m)=\widetilde{k}_{1}(|n|,\mp|m|)
and
\widetilde{k}_{1}(-n,-m)=-\widetilde{k}_{1}(n,m)
, we have

\displaystyle k^{\pm}_{1}(n,m)

\displaystyle=(\chi_{+}(n)+\chi_{-}(n))k^{\pm}_{1}(n,m)(\chi_{+}(m)+\chi_{-}(m))

\displaystyle=\chi_{+}(n)\widetilde{k}_{1}(n,\mp m)\chi_{+}(m)+\chi_{+}(n)\widetilde{k}_{1}(n,\pm m)\chi_{-}(m)

\displaystyle\quad-\chi_{-}(n)\widetilde{k}_{1}(n,\pm m)\chi_{+}(m)-\chi_{-}(n)\widetilde{k}_{1}(n,\mp m)\chi_{-}(m).

Then equation (
3.55
) follows by making the change of variable
m\mapsto-m
in the first and fourth terms for the
``+"
case, and the second and third terms for the
``-"
case, respectively. Similarly, we can obtain

\displaystyle k^{\pm}_{2}(n,m)

\displaystyle=\chi_{+}(n)\widetilde{k}^{\pm}_{2}(n,m)\chi_{+}(m)+\chi_{+}(n)\widetilde{k}^{\pm}_{2}(n,-m)\chi_{-}(m)

\displaystyle\quad-\chi_{-}(n)\widetilde{k}^{\pm}_{2}(n,-m)\chi_{+}(m)-\chi_{-}(n)\widetilde{k}^{\pm}_{2}(n,m)\chi_{-}(m).

Applying the variable substitution
m\mapsto-m
in the second and third terms yields (
3.56
). Since
[
44
, Lemma 3.3]
has established that
T_{\widetilde{k}_{1}}
and
T_{\widetilde{k}^{\pm}_{2}}
are Calderón-Zygmund operators, thus by Theorem
A.1
, it follows that
\widetilde{k}_{1}
and
\widetilde{k}^{\pm}_{2}
are
\ell^{p}
bounded for
1<p<\infty
. We then get the desired result.
∎

4.
The intermediate energy part
\mathcal{K}_{2}

Theorem 4.1
.

Let
H=\Delta^{2}+V
with
|V(n)|\lesssim\left<n\right>^{-\beta}
for
\beta>3
. Suppose that
H
has no positive eigenvalues in the interval
\rm{(}0,16\rm{)}
, then
\mathcal{K}_{2}\in\mathbb{B}(\ell^{p}(\mathbb{Z}))
for
1\leq p\leq\infty
.

Proof.

Recall from (
1.10
) and by virtue of the identity

R^{+}_{V}(\mu^{4})=R^{+}_{0}(\mu^{4})-R^{+}_{0}(\mu^{4})VR^{+}_{V}(\mu^{4}),

the kernel of
\mathcal{K}_{2}
is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{2}(n,m)=(\mathcal{K}_{21}-\mathcal{K}_{22})(n,m),

where

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{K}_{21}(n,m)&=\int_{0}^{2}\mu^{3}\chi_{2}(\mu)\big[R^{+}_{0}(\mu^{4})V(R^{+}_{0}-R^{-}_{0})(\mu^{4})\big](n,m)d\mu,\\
\mathcal{K}_{22}(n,m)&=\int_{0}^{2}\mu^{3}\chi_{2}(\mu)\big[R^{+}_{0}(\mu^{4})VR^{+}_{V}(\mu^{4})V(R^{+}_{0}-R^{-}_{0})(\mu^{4})\big](n,m)d\mu.\end{split}

(4.1)

Next, we claim that both kernels
\mathcal{K}_{2j}(j=1,2)
satisfy the estimate

\displaystyle|\mathcal{K}_{2j}(n,m)|\lesssim\left<|n|\pm|m|\right>^{-2}.

(4.2)

Combining this with Lemma
3.4
, we conclude that
\mathcal{K}_{2}\in\mathbb{B}(\ell^{p}(\mathbb{Z}))
for all
1\leq p\leq\infty
.

(1) For
{\bm{j=1}}
, it follows from (
4.1
) and (
2.4
) that

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{21}(n,m)=\frac{1}{16}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{2}(\mathcal{K}^{+,j}_{21}+\mathcal{K}^{-,j}_{21})(n,m),

(4.3)

where
N_{1}=n-m_{1}
,
M_{1}=m-m_{1}
and

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{\pm,1}_{21}(n,m)

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{2}\mu^{-3}(a_{1}(\mu))^{2}\chi_{2}(\mu)\sum\limits_{m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-i\theta_{+}(|N_{1}|\pm|M_{1}|)}V(m_{1})d\mu,

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{\pm,2}_{21}(n,m)

\displaystyle=i\int_{0}^{2}\mu^{-3}a_{1}(\mu)a_{2}(\mu)\chi_{2}(\mu)\sum\limits_{m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{b(\mu)|N_{1}|\pm i\theta_{+}|M_{1}|}V(m_{1})d\mu.

By applying the argument for
K^{\pm,j}_{0}
in Proposition
3.5
to
\mathcal{K}^{\pm,j}_{21}
, while noting that
supp\chi_{2}(\mu)\subseteq[\mu_{0},2-\mu_{0}]
, it is not difficult to obtain

\displaystyle|\mathcal{K}^{\pm,j}_{21}(n,m)|\lesssim\left<|n|\pm|m|\right>^{-2},\quad j=1,2,

which establishes (
4.2
) for
\mathcal{K}_{21}
.

(2) For
{\bm{j=2}}
, by (
4.1
) and (
2.4
), we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{22}(n,m)=\frac{1}{16}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{2}(\mathcal{K}^{+,j}_{22}+\mathcal{K}^{-,j}_{22})(n,m),

where
N_{1}=n-m_{1}
,
M_{2}=m-m_{2}
and

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{\pm,1}_{22}(n,m)

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{2}e^{-i\theta_{+}(|n|\pm|m|)}\mu^{-3}(a_{1}(\mu))^{2}\chi_{2}(\mu)L^{\pm,1}_{22}(\mu,n,m)d\mu,

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{\pm,2}_{22}(n,m)

\displaystyle=i\int_{0}^{2}e^{b(\mu)|n|\pm i\theta_{+}|m|}\mu^{-3}a_{1}(\mu)a_{2}(\mu)\chi_{2}(\mu)L^{\pm,2}_{22}(\mu,n,m)d\mu,

with

\displaystyle L^{\pm,1}_{22}(\mu,n,m)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-i\theta_{+}(|N_{1}|-|n|\pm(|M_{2}|-|m|))}(VR^{+}_{V}(\mu^{4})V)(m_{1},m_{2}),

\displaystyle L^{\pm,2}_{22}(\mu,n,m)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{b(\mu)(|N_{1}|-|n|)\pm i\theta_{+}(|M_{2}|-|m|)}(VR^{+}_{V}(\mu^{4})V)(m_{1},m_{2}).

We shall show that for any
k=0,1,2
, the following estimates hold:

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{\mu\in[\mu_{0},2-\mu_{0}]}\Big|\big(\partial^{k}_{\mu}L^{\pm,1}_{22}\big)(\mu,n,m)\Big|+\sup\limits_{\mu\in[\mu_{0},2-\mu_{0}]}\Big|e^{b(\mu)|n|}\big(\partial^{k}_{\mu}L^{\pm,2}_{22}\big)(\mu,n,m)\Big|\lesssim 1,

(4.4)

uniformly in
n,m\in\mathbb{Z}
. With this established, using
supp\chi_{2}(\mu)\subseteq[\mu_{0},2-\mu_{0}]
and applying the method used for
K^{\pm,j}_{0}
to
\mathcal{K}^{\pm,j}_{22}
, we can derive

\displaystyle|\mathcal{K}^{\pm,j}_{22}(n,m)|\lesssim\left<|n|\pm|m|\right>^{-2},\quad j=1,2,

which gives (
4.2
). To obtain (
4.4
), we focus on
L^{\pm,2}_{22}
(the case for
L^{\pm,1}_{22}
being similar). For
k=0,1,2
,

\displaystyle\big(\partial^{k}_{\mu}L^{\pm,2}_{22}\big)(\mu,n,m)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{k_{1}=0}^{k}\sum\limits_{m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}C^{k_{1}}_{k}\partial^{k-k_{1}}_{\mu}\big(e^{b(\mu)(|N_{1}|-|n|)\pm i\theta_{+}(|M_{2}|-|m|)}\big)(V\partial^{k_{1}}_{\mu}(R^{+}_{V}(\mu^{4}))V)(m_{1},m_{2})

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{k_{1}=0}^{k}\sum\limits_{m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}C^{k_{1}}_{k}\partial^{k-k_{1}}_{\mu}\big(e^{b(\mu)(|N_{1}|-|n|)\pm i\theta_{+}(|M_{2}|-|m|)}\big)V(m_{1})\left<m_{1}\right>^{\varepsilon_{k_{1}}}

(4.5)

\displaystyle\quad\times\big(\left<\cdot\right>^{-\varepsilon_{k_{1}}}\partial^{k_{1}}_{\mu}(R^{+}_{V}(\mu^{4}))\left<\cdot\right>^{-\varepsilon_{k_{1}}}\big)(m_{1},m_{2})\left<m_{2}\right>^{\varepsilon_{k_{1}}}V(m_{2}),

where
\varepsilon_{k_{1}}
is a positive constant depending on
k_{1}
specified later.
Noting that both
b^{\prime}(\mu)
and
\theta^{\prime}_{+}(\mu)
are smooth on
[\mu_{0},2-\mu_{0}]
, and
b(\mu),b^{\prime}(\mu)<0
, we have

\sup\limits_{N_{1},M_{2},m}\sup\limits_{\mu\in[\mu_{0},2-\mu_{0}]}\big|e^{b(\mu)|N_{1}|\pm i\theta_{+}(|M_{2}|-|m|)}\big|\lesssim 1.

These facts together with Lemma
2.2
(taking
k_{1}+\frac{1}{2}<\varepsilon_{k_{1}}<\beta-\frac{1}{2}+k_{1}-k
in (
4
)) yields

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{n,m\in\mathbb{Z}}\sup\limits_{\mu\in[\mu_{0},2-\mu_{0}]}\Big|e^{b(\mu)|n|}\big(\partial^{k}_{\mu}L^{\pm,2}_{22}\big)(\mu,n,m)\Big|\lesssim 1,\quad k=0,1,2.

Therefore, (
4.4
) is obtained and this completes the whole proof.
∎

5.
The high energy part
\mathcal{K}_{3}

Theorem 5.1
.

Let
H=\Delta^{2}+V
with
|V(n)|\lesssim\left<n\right>^{-\beta}
for some
\beta>0
. Suppose that
H
has no positive eigenvalues in the interval
\rm{(}0,16\rm{)}
. If

\displaystyle\beta>\left\{\begin{aligned} &9,\ 16\ is\ a\ regular\ point\ of\ H,\\
&13,\ 16\ is\ a\ resonance\ of\ H,\\
&17,\ 16\ is\ an\ eigenvalue\ of\ H,\end{aligned}\right.

then
\mathcal{K}_{3}\in\mathbb{B}(\ell^{p}(\mathbb{Z}))
for all
1<p<\infty
.

Prior to the proof, we first recall that

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{3}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{2}\mu^{3}\chi_{3}(\mu)\big[R^{+}_{0}(\mu^{4})vM^{-1}(\mu)v(R^{+}_{0}-R^{-}_{0})(\mu^{4})\big]d\mu.

(5.1)

We remark that an important difference from Section
3
is that it is not straightforward to utilize the cancelation properties of the projection operators
\widetilde{Q}
,
\widetilde{S}_{0}
,
\widetilde{S}_{1}
in the expansions of
M^{-1}(\mu)
to eliminate the singularity at
\mu=2
. To overcome this difficulty, we resort to the unitary operator
J
(defined in (
2.7
)), which can transfer the operator
R^{+}_{0}(\mu^{4})vBv(R^{+}_{0}-R^{-}_{0})(\mu^{4})
to the form

R^{+}_{0}(\mu^{4})vBv(R^{+}_{0}-R^{-}_{0})(\mu^{4})=\frac{1}{4\mu^{4}}J\big(R^{-}_{-\Delta}(4-\mu^{2})+JR_{-\Delta}(-\mu^{2})J\big)\tilde{v}B\tilde{v}(R^{-}_{-\Delta}-R^{+}_{-\Delta})(4-\mu^{2})J

(5.2)

via the relation
J^{2}=I
and the formulas

JR^{\pm}_{-\Delta}(\mu^{2})J=-R^{\mp}_{-\Delta}(4-\mu^{2}),\quad R^{\pm}_{0}(\mu^{4})=\frac{1}{2\mu^{2}}\left(R^{\pm}_{-\Delta}(\mu^{2})-R_{-\Delta}(-\mu^{2})\right),\quad\mu\in(0,2).

This form indicates that one can turn to establish the following lemma to eliminate the singularity.

Lemma 5.2
.

(
[
28
, Lemma 4.9 and Lemma 4.14]
)

Let
\widetilde{Q}
,
\widetilde{S}_{0}
,
\widetilde{S}_{1}
be as in Definition
2.5
. For any
f\in\ell^{2}(\mathbb{Z})
, then we have

(1)

\big(R^{\mp}_{-\Delta}(4-\mu^{2})\tilde{v}Wf\big)(n)=(2{\rm sin}\tilde{\theta}_{+})^{-1}\sum\limits_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{0}^{1}{\bm{\tilde{\theta}_{+}}}({\rm sign}(n-\rho m))e^{\pm i\tilde{\theta}_{+}|n-\rho m|}d\rho\cdot\tilde{v}_{1}(m)(Wf)(m)
,

:=(2{\rm sin}\tilde{\theta}_{+})^{-1}\sum\limits_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{\pm}(\tilde{\theta}_{+},n,m)(Wf)(m)
,
W=\widetilde{Q}
,
\widetilde{S}_{0}
,
\widetilde{S}_{1}
,

(2)

\big[\big((R^{-}_{-\Delta}-R^{+}_{-\Delta})(4-\mu^{2})\big)\tilde{v}\widetilde{S}_{1}f\big](n)=(2{\rm sin}\tilde{\theta}_{+})^{-1}\sum\limits_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{0}^{1}i{\bm{\tilde{\theta}^{2}_{+}}}(\rho-1)(e^{i\tilde{\theta}_{+}|n-\rho m|}+e^{-i\tilde{\theta}_{+}|n-\rho m|})d\rho

\times\tilde{v}_{2}(m)(\widetilde{S}_{1}f)(m)
,

:=(2{\rm sin}\tilde{\theta}_{+})^{-1}\sum\limits_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}(\tilde{\theta}_{+},n,m)(\widetilde{S}_{1}f)(m)
,

(3)

W\big(\tilde{v}R^{\mp}_{-\Delta}(4-\mu^{2})f\big)=W\widetilde{f}^{\pm}
,
\widetilde{S}_{1}\big(\tilde{v}\big((R^{-}_{-\Delta}-R^{+}_{-\Delta})(4-\mu^{2})\big)f\big)=\widetilde{S}_{1}\widetilde{f}_{1}
,

where
\tilde{\theta}_{+}:=\tilde{\theta}_{+}(\mu)
satisfies
{\rm cos}\tilde{\theta}_{+}=\frac{\mu^{2}}{2}-1
with
\tilde{\theta}_{+}\in(-\pi,0)
and

\quad\widetilde{f}^{\pm}(n)=(2{\rm sin}\tilde{\theta}_{+})^{-1}\sum\limits_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{\pm}(\tilde{\theta}_{+},m,n)f(m),\quad\widetilde{f}_{1}(n)=(2{\rm sin}\tilde{\theta}_{+})^{-1}\sum\limits_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}(\tilde{\theta}_{+},m,n)f(m).

Remark 5.3
.

Noting that
\tilde{\theta}_{+}=O((2-\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}})
as
\mu\rightarrow 2
, compared with
R^{\mp}_{-\Delta}(4-\mu^{2})=O((2-\mu)^{-\frac{1}{2}})
, this lemma indicates that these operators can eliminate the singularity of
R^{\mp}_{-\Delta}(4-\mu^{2})
. Precisely,

\displaystyle\begin{split}R^{\mp}_{-\Delta}(4-\mu^{2})\tilde{v}W&=O(1),\quad\big((R^{-}_{-\Delta}-R^{+}_{-\Delta})(4-\mu^{2})\big)\tilde{v}\widetilde{S}_{1}=O((2-\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}),\quad W=\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{S}_{0},\widetilde{S}_{1},\\
W\tilde{v}R^{\mp}_{-\Delta}(4-\mu^{2})&=O(1),\quad\widetilde{S}_{1}\big(\tilde{v}\big((R^{-}_{-\Delta}-R^{+}_{-\Delta})(4-\mu^{2})\big)\big)=O((2-\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}).\end{split}

(5.3)

To prove Theorem
5.1
, we will address each case individually in the following three subsections.

5.1.
16
is a regular point of
H

In this subsection, we prove the
\ell^{p}
boundedness for
\mathcal{K}_{3}
when
16
is a regular point of
H
. Recall the expansion (
2.11
) of
M^{-1}(\mu)
as
\mu\rightarrow 2
:

\displaystyle M^{-1}(\mu)=\widetilde{Q}B_{0}\widetilde{Q}+(2-\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}(\widetilde{Q}B^{0}_{11}+B^{0}_{12}\widetilde{Q})+(2-\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}\widetilde{P}_{1}+(2-\mu)B^{0}_{21}+\Gamma^{0}_{\frac{3}{2}}(2-\mu)

and substitute it into (
5.1
), then
\mathcal{K}_{3}
can be expressed as the sum of six integral operators:

\mathcal{K}_{3}=\sum\limits_{B\in\mathcal{B}_{0}}K_{B}+K_{\widetilde{P}_{1}}+K^{0}_{r},

(5.4)

where
\mathcal{B}_{0}=\{\widetilde{Q}B_{0}\widetilde{Q},(2-\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}\widetilde{Q}B^{0}_{11},(2-\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}B^{0}_{12}\widetilde{Q},(2-\mu)B^{0}_{21}\}
and

\displaystyle K_{B}(n,m)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{2}\mu^{3}\chi_{3}(\mu)\big[R^{+}_{0}(\mu^{4})vBv(R^{+}_{0}-R^{-}_{0})(\mu^{4})\big](n,m)d\mu,\quad B\in\mathcal{B}_{0}

\displaystyle K_{\widetilde{P}_{1}}(n,m)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{2}(2-\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}\mu^{3}\chi_{3}(\mu)\big[R^{+}_{0}(\mu^{4})v\widetilde{P}_{1}v(R^{+}_{0}-R^{-}_{0})(\mu^{4})\big](n,m)d\mu,

(5.5)

\displaystyle K^{0}_{r}(n,m)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{2}\mu^{3}\chi_{3}(\mu)\big[R^{+}_{0}(\mu^{4})v\Gamma^{0}_{\frac{3}{2}}(2-\mu)v(R^{+}_{0}-R^{-}_{0})(\mu^{4})\big](n,m)d\mu.

(5.6)

Based on (
5.2
) and (
5.3
), we can further classify these operators as the following three types according to their order in
(2-\mu)
as
\mu\rightarrow 2
:

\displaystyle O(1):K_{B}(B\in\mathcal{B}_{0}),\quad O((2-\mu)^{-\frac{1}{2}}):K_{\widetilde{P}_{1}},\quad O((2-\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}):K^{0}_{r}.

Proposition 5.4
.

Let
H=\Delta^{2}+V
with
|V(n)|\lesssim\left<n\right>^{-\beta}
for
\beta>9
. Suppose that
H
has no positive eigenvalues in the interval
\rm{(}0,16\rm{)}
and
16
is a regular point of
H
. Let
\mathcal{B}_{0}
be defined in (
5.4
). Then

(1)
for any
K\in\{K^{0}_{r}\}\cup\{K_{B}:B\in\mathcal{B}_{0}\}
,
K\in\mathbb{B}(\ell^{p}(\mathbb{Z}))
for any
1\leq p\leq\infty
,

(2)

K_{\widetilde{P}_{1}}\in\mathbb{B}(\ell^{p}(\mathbb{Z}))
for all
1<p<\infty
.

Therefore,
\mathcal{K}_{3}\in\mathbb{B}(\ell^{p}(\mathbb{Z}))
for any
1<p<\infty
.

Proof.

(1)

Step 1:
For any
B\in\mathcal{B}_{0}
, denote

\mathcal{K}_{B}(\mu,n,m)=16\mu^{3}\big(R^{+}_{0}(\mu^{4})vBv(R^{+}_{0}-R^{-}_{0})(\mu^{4})\big)(n,m).

It follows from (
5.2
) and Lemma
5.2
that

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{B}(\mu,n,m)=\left\{\begin{aligned} &\sum\limits_{m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big[\int_{[0,1]^{2}}(-1)^{n+m}\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{(1)}_{0}(N_{1},M_{2},m_{1},m_{2})(f_{0}^{+,1}-f_{0}^{-,1})(\mu,N_{1},M_{2})d\rho_{1}d\rho_{2}\\
&\quad+\int_{0}^{1}(-1)^{m}\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{(2)}_{0}(M_{2},m_{1},m_{2})(f_{0}^{+,2}-f_{0}^{-,2})(\mu,\widetilde{N}_{1},M_{2})d\rho_{2}\Big],\ \ B=\widetilde{Q}B_{0}\widetilde{Q},\\
&\sum\limits_{m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big[\int_{0}^{1}(-1)^{n+m}\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{(1)}_{11}(N_{1},m_{1},m_{2})\big(f^{+,1}_{11}+f^{-,1}_{11}\big)(\mu,N_{1},\widetilde{M}_{2})d\rho_{1}+\\
&\quad+(-1)^{m}\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{(2)}_{11}(m_{1},m_{2})\big(f^{+,2}_{11}+f^{-,2}_{11}\big)(\mu,\widetilde{N}_{1},\widetilde{M}_{2})\Big],\quad B=(2-\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}\widetilde{Q}B^{0}_{11},\\
&\sum\limits_{m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{0}^{1}\Big[(-1)^{n+m}\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{(1)}_{12}(M_{2},m_{1},m_{2})(f^{+,1}_{12}-f^{-,1}_{12})(\mu,\widetilde{N}_{1},M_{2})+(-1)^{m}\times\\
&\qquad\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{(2)}_{12}(M_{2},m_{1},m_{2})(f^{+,2}_{12}-f^{-,2}_{12})(\mu,\widetilde{N}_{1},M_{2})\Big]d\rho_{2},\quad B=(2-\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}B^{0}_{12}\widetilde{Q},\\
&\sum\limits_{m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big[(-1)^{n+m}\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{(1)}_{21}(m_{1},m_{2})((f^{+,1}_{21}+f^{-,1}_{21})(\mu,\widetilde{N}_{1},\widetilde{M}_{2}))\\
&\quad\qquad+(-1)^{m}\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{(2)}_{21}(m_{1},m_{2})((f^{+,2}_{21}+f^{-,2}_{21})(\mu,\widetilde{N}_{1},\widetilde{M}_{2}))\Big],\quad B=(2-\mu)B^{0}_{21},\end{aligned}\right.

(5.7)

where
N_{1}=n-\rho_{1}m_{1}
,
\widetilde{N}_{1}=n-m_{1}
,
M_{2}=m-\rho_{2}m_{2}
,
\widetilde{M}_{2}=m-m_{2}
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Phi}^{\pm}_{1}(\mu,X,Y)

\displaystyle=e^{i\tilde{\theta}_{+}(|X|\pm|Y|)},\quad\widetilde{\Phi}^{\pm}_{2}(\mu,X,Y)=e^{b(\mu)|X|\pm i\tilde{\theta}_{+}|Y|},

\displaystyle f^{\pm,1}_{0}(\mu,N_{1},M_{2})

\displaystyle=\frac{\tilde{\theta}^{2}_{+}}{\mu({\rm sin}\tilde{\theta}_{+})^{2}}\widetilde{\Phi}^{\pm}_{1}(\mu,N_{1},M_{2}),\quad f^{\pm,2}_{0}(\mu,\widetilde{N}_{1},M_{2})=\frac{-a_{2}(\mu)\tilde{\theta}_{+}}{\mu^{2}{\rm sin}\tilde{\theta}_{+}}\widetilde{\Phi}^{\pm}_{2}(\mu,\widetilde{N}_{1},M_{2}),

\displaystyle f^{\pm,1}_{11}(\mu,N_{1},\widetilde{M}_{2})

\displaystyle=\frac{i(2-\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}\tilde{\theta}_{+}}{\mu({\rm sin}\tilde{\theta}_{+})^{2}}\widetilde{\Phi}^{\pm}_{1}(\mu,N_{1},\widetilde{M}_{2}),\quad f^{\pm,1}_{12}(\mu,\widetilde{N}_{1},{M}_{2})=f^{\pm,1}_{11}(\mu,\widetilde{N}_{1},{M}_{2}),

\displaystyle f^{\pm,2}_{11}(\mu,\widetilde{N}_{1},\widetilde{M}_{2})

\displaystyle=\frac{-ia_{2}(\mu)(2-\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\mu^{2}{\rm sin}\tilde{\theta}_{+}}\widetilde{\Phi}^{\pm}_{2}(\mu,\widetilde{N}_{1},\widetilde{M}_{2}),\quad f^{\pm,2}_{12}(\mu,\widetilde{N}_{1},{M}_{2})=-i\tilde{\theta}_{+}f^{\pm,2}_{11}(\mu,\widetilde{N}_{1},{M}_{2}),

\displaystyle f^{\pm,1}_{21}(\mu,\widetilde{N}_{1},\widetilde{M}_{2})

\displaystyle=\frac{\mu-2}{\mu({\rm sin}\tilde{\theta}_{+})^{2}}\widetilde{\Phi}^{\pm}_{1}(\mu,\widetilde{N}_{1},\widetilde{M}_{2}),\quad f^{\pm,2}_{21}(\mu,\widetilde{N}_{1},\widetilde{M}_{2})=\frac{ia_{2}(\mu)(\mu-2)}{\mu^{2}{\rm sin}\tilde{\theta}_{+}}\widetilde{\Phi}^{\pm}_{2}(\mu,\widetilde{N}_{1},\widetilde{M}_{2}),

and

•

\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{(1)}_{0}(N_{1},M_{2},m_{1},m_{2})=({\rm sign}(N_{1}))({\rm sign}(M_{2}))(\tilde{v}_{1}\widetilde{Q}B_{0}\widetilde{Q}\tilde{v}_{1})(m_{1},m_{2}),

•

\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{(2)}_{0}(M_{2},m_{1},m_{2})=({\rm sign}(M_{2}))(v\widetilde{Q}B_{0}\widetilde{Q}\tilde{v}_{1})(m_{1},m_{2}),

•

\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{(1)}_{11}(N_{1},m_{1},m_{2})=({\rm sign}(N_{1}))(\tilde{v}_{1}\widetilde{Q}B^{0}_{11}\tilde{v})(m_{1},m_{2})
,
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{(2)}_{11}(m_{1},m_{2})=({v}\widetilde{Q}B^{0}_{11}\tilde{{v}})(m_{1},m_{2})
,

•

\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{(1)}_{12}(M_{2},m_{1},m_{2})=({\rm sign}(M_{2}))(\tilde{v}B^{0}_{12}\widetilde{Q}\tilde{v}_{1})(m_{1},m_{2})
,
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{(1)}_{21}(m_{1},m_{2})=(\tilde{v}B^{0}_{21}\tilde{v})(m_{1},m_{2})
,

•

\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{(2)}_{12}(M_{2},m_{1},m_{2})=({\rm sign}(M_{2}))({v}B^{0}_{12}\widetilde{Q}\tilde{v}_{1})(m_{1},m_{2})
,
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{(2)}_{21}(m_{1},m_{2})=({v}B^{0}_{21}\tilde{v})(m_{1},m_{2})
.

In view that
\mathcal{K}_{B}(\mu,n,m)=O(1)
as
\mu\rightarrow 2
for any
B\in\mathcal{B}_{0}
, next we consider the case
B=\widetilde{Q}B_{0}\widetilde{Q}
only and other terms can be derived similarly. Let

\widetilde{K}_{0}(n,m)=\int_{0}^{2}\mu^{3}\chi_{3}(\mu)\big[R^{+}_{0}(\mu^{4})v\widetilde{Q}B_{0}\widetilde{Q}v(R^{+}_{0}-R^{-}_{0})(\mu^{4})\big](n,m)d\mu.

(5.8)

From (
5.7
), it reduces to establish the boundedness of the following two operators:

\displaystyle\widetilde{K}^{\pm,1}_{0}(n,m)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{n+m}\int_{0}^{2}e^{i\tilde{\theta}_{+}(|n|\pm|m|)}\frac{\tilde{\theta}^{2}_{+}\chi_{3}(\mu)}{\mu({\rm sin}\tilde{\theta}_{+})^{2}}\widetilde{L}^{\pm,1}_{0}(\tilde{\theta}_{+},n,m)d\mu,

(5.9)

\displaystyle\widetilde{K}^{\pm,2}_{0}(n,m)

\displaystyle=-(-1)^{m}\int_{0}^{2}e^{\pm i\tilde{\theta}_{+}(|m|\pm i|n|)}\frac{a_{2}(\mu)\tilde{\theta}_{+}}{\mu^{2}{\rm sin}\tilde{\theta}_{+}}\chi_{3}(\mu)\widetilde{L}^{\pm,2}_{0}(\mu,n,m)d\mu,

(5.10)

where

\displaystyle\widetilde{L}^{\pm,1}_{0}(\tilde{\theta}_{+},n,m)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{[0,1]^{2}}\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{(1)}_{0}(N_{1},M_{2},m_{1},m_{2})e^{i\tilde{\theta}_{+}\big(|N_{1}|-|n|\pm(|M_{2}|-|m|)\big)}d\rho_{1}d\rho_{2},

\displaystyle\widetilde{L}^{\pm,2}_{0}(\mu,n,m)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{0}^{1}\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{(2)}_{0}(M_{2},m_{1},m_{2})e^{b(\mu)|\widetilde{N}_{1}|+\tilde{\theta}_{+}|n|\pm i\tilde{\theta}_{+}(|M_{2}|-|m|)}d\rho_{2}.

Applying the variable substitution to
\widetilde{K}^{\pm,j}_{0}
for
j=1,2

\displaystyle{\rm cos}\tilde{\theta}_{+}=\frac{\mu^{2}}{2}-1\Longrightarrow\ \frac{d\mu}{d\tilde{\theta}_{+}}=-\frac{{\rm sin}\tilde{\theta}_{+}}{\mu},\quad\tilde{\theta}_{+}\rightarrow-\pi\ {\rm as}\ \mu\rightarrow 0\ {\rm and}\ \tilde{\theta}_{+}\rightarrow 0\ {\rm as}\ \mu\rightarrow 2,

(5.11)

we further obtain

\displaystyle\widetilde{K}^{\pm,1}_{0}(n,m)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{n+m}\int_{-\pi}^{0}e^{i\tilde{\theta}_{+}(|n|\pm|m|)}\frac{\tilde{\theta}^{2}_{+}}{{\rm sin}\tilde{\theta}_{+}}\tilde{\chi}_{3}(\mu(\tilde{\theta}_{+}))\widetilde{L}^{\pm,1}_{0}(\tilde{\theta}_{+},n,m)d\tilde{\theta}_{+},

\displaystyle\widetilde{K}^{\pm,2}_{0}(n,m)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{m}\int_{-\pi}^{0}e^{\pm i\tilde{\theta}_{+}(|m|\pm i|n|)}\tilde{\tilde{\chi}}_{3}(\mu(\tilde{\theta}_{+}))\tilde{\theta}_{+}\widetilde{L}^{\pm,2}_{0}(\mu(\tilde{\theta}_{+}),n,m)d\tilde{\theta}_{+},

where
\tilde{\chi}_{3}(\mu)=-\mu^{-2}\chi_{3}(\mu)
and
\tilde{\tilde{\chi}}_{3}(\mu)=-\mu^{-3}\big(1+\frac{\mu^{2}}{4}\big)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\chi_{3}(\mu)
. It is clearly that for any
k=0,1,2
,

\sup\limits_{\tilde{\theta}_{+}\in(-\pi,0)}\big|(\partial^{k}_{\tilde{\theta}_{+}}\widetilde{L}^{\pm,1}_{0})(\tilde{\theta}_{+},n,m)\big|\lesssim\|\left<\cdot\right>^{2k+2}V(\cdot)\|_{\ell^{1}},\quad{\rm uniformly\ in}\ n,m\in\mathbb{Z}.

Next, we verify that the following estimates hold:

\sup\limits_{\tilde{\theta}_{+}\in[\gamma_{1},0)}\big|(\partial^{k}_{\tilde{\theta}_{+}}\widetilde{L}^{\pm,2}_{0})(\mu(\tilde{\theta}_{+}),n,m)\big|\lesssim\|\left<\cdot\right>^{2k+2}V(\cdot)\|_{\ell^{1}},\quad{\rm uniformly\ in}\ n,m\in\mathbb{Z},

(5.12)

where
\gamma_{1}\in(-\pi,0)
satisfying
{\rm cos}\gamma_{1}=\frac{(2-\mu_{0})^{2}}{2}-1
. Combining these estimates with arguments analogous to those used for
K^{\pm,1}_{0}
, we immediately conclude that
\widetilde{K}^{\pm,j}_{0}\in\mathbb{B}(\ell^{p}(\mathbb{Z}))
for
1\leq p\leq\infty
and
j=1,2
.

To see this, we first observe that
b(\mu)<0
and
b^{\prime}(\mu)<0
on the interval
(0,2)
, which implies that for any
k\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{\mu\in[2-\mu_{0},2)}|N_{1}|^{k}e^{b(\mu)|N_{1}|}\leq\sup\limits_{N_{1}}|N_{1}|^{k}e^{b(2-\mu_{0})|N_{1}|}<\infty.

(5.13)

This estimate immediately verifies (
5.12
) for
k=0
. For the cases
k=1,2
, we can calculate

\displaystyle(\partial^{k}_{\tilde{\theta}_{+}}\widetilde{L}^{\pm,2}_{0})(\mu(\tilde{\theta}_{+}),n,m)=\sum\limits_{m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{0}^{1}\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{(2)}_{0}(M_{2},m_{1},m_{2})\underbrace{\partial^{k}_{\tilde{\theta}_{+}}\big(e^{b(\mu(\tilde{\theta}_{+}))|\widetilde{N}_{1}|+\tilde{\theta}_{+}|n|\pm i\tilde{\theta}_{+}(|M_{2}|-|m|)}\big)}_{:=\mathcal{L}_{k}(\tilde{\theta}_{+},\widetilde{N}_{1},n,M_{2},m)}d\rho_{2}

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{1}(\tilde{\theta}_{+},\widetilde{N}_{1},n,M_{2},m)

\displaystyle=\big[(b^{\prime}(\mu(\tilde{\theta}_{+}))\mu^{\prime}(\tilde{\theta}_{+})+1)|\widetilde{N}_{1}|+|n|-|\widetilde{N}_{1}|\pm i(|M_{2}|-|m|)\big]

\displaystyle\quad\times e^{b(\mu(\tilde{\theta}_{+}))|\widetilde{N}_{1}|+\tilde{\theta}_{+}|n|\pm i\tilde{\theta}_{+}(|M_{2}|-|m|)},

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{2}(\tilde{\theta}_{+},\widetilde{N}_{1},n,M_{2},m)

\displaystyle=\Big(\big[(b^{\prime}(\mu(\tilde{\theta}_{+}))\mu^{\prime}(\tilde{\theta}_{+})+1)|\widetilde{N}_{1}|+|n|-|\widetilde{N}_{1}|\pm i(|M_{2}|-|m|)\big]^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+(b^{\prime}(\mu(\tilde{\theta}_{+}))\mu^{\prime}(\tilde{\theta}_{+}))^{\prime}|\widetilde{N}_{1}|\Big)\times e^{b(\mu(\tilde{\theta}_{+}))|\widetilde{N}_{1}|+\tilde{\theta}_{+}|n|\pm i\tilde{\theta}_{+}(|M_{2}|-|m|)}.

Combining this with (
5.13
),
\tilde{\theta}_{+}<0
and the continuous differentiability of
b^{\prime}(\mu)
and
\mu^{\prime}(\tilde{\theta}_{+})
:

b^{\prime}(\mu)=-(2+\mu^{2})^{-1}\big((4+\mu^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}+\mu^{2}(4+\mu^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big),\quad\mu^{\prime}(\tilde{\theta}_{+})=\big(1-\frac{\mu^{2}}{4}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}},\quad\mu^{\prime\prime}(\tilde{\theta}_{+})=-\frac{\mu}{4},

we obtain

\sup\limits_{\tilde{\theta}_{+}\in[\gamma_{1},0)}|\mathcal{L}_{k}(\tilde{\theta}_{+},\widetilde{N}_{1},n,M_{2},m)|\lesssim\left<m_{1}\right>^{k}\left<m_{2}\right>^{k},\quad{\rm uniformly\ in}\ \widetilde{N}_{1},M_{2},n,m.

Hence, by Hölder’s inequality, the desired estimate (
5.12
) is obtained.

Step 2:
For
K=K^{0}_{r}
, it follows from (
5.6
) that

\displaystyle K^{0}_{r}(n,m)=\frac{1}{16}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{2}(K^{+}_{rj}+K^{-}_{rj})(n,m),

where

\displaystyle K^{\pm}_{r1}(n,m)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{n+m}\int_{0}^{2}\frac{-\chi_{3}(\mu)}{\mu({\rm sin}\tilde{\theta}_{+})^{2}}\sum\limits_{m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\widetilde{\Phi}^{\pm}_{1}(\mu,\widetilde{N}_{1},\widetilde{M}_{2})(\tilde{v}\Gamma^{0}_{\frac{3}{2}}(2-\mu)\tilde{v})(m_{1},m_{2})d\mu,

\displaystyle{\mathrel{\to}^{\eqref{varible substi2}}}

\displaystyle(-1)^{n+m}\int_{-\pi}^{0}e^{i\tilde{\theta}_{+}(|n|\pm|m|)}\sum\limits_{m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{i\tilde{\theta}_{+}(|\widetilde{N}_{1}|-|n|\pm(|\widetilde{M}_{2}|-|m|))}(\tilde{v}\varphi_{1}(\mu(\tilde{\theta}_{+}))\tilde{v})(m_{1},m_{2})d\tilde{\theta}_{+},

\displaystyle K^{\pm}_{r2}(n,m)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{m}\int_{0}^{2}\frac{-ia_{2}(\mu)}{\mu^{2}{\rm sin}\tilde{\theta}_{+}}\chi_{3}(\mu)\sum\limits_{m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\widetilde{\Phi}^{\pm}_{2}(\mu,\widetilde{N}_{1},\widetilde{M}_{2})({v}\Gamma^{0}_{\frac{3}{2}}(2-\mu)\tilde{v})(m_{1},m_{2})d\mu,

\displaystyle{\mathrel{\to}^{\eqref{varible substi2}}}(-1)

{}^{m}\int_{-\pi}^{0}e^{\pm i\tilde{\theta}_{+}(|m|\pm i|n|)}\sum\limits_{m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{b(\mu(\tilde{\theta}_{+}))|\widetilde{N}_{1}|+\tilde{\theta}_{+}|n|\pm i\tilde{\theta}_{+}(|\widetilde{M}_{2}|-|m|))}(v\varphi_{2}(\mu(\tilde{\theta}_{+}))\tilde{v})(m_{1},m_{2})d\tilde{\theta}_{+},

with
\Gamma(\mu)=\frac{\Gamma^{0}_{\frac{3}{2}}(2-\mu)}{(2-\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}},\ \varphi_{1}(\mu)=-2\mu^{-3}(2+\mu)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\chi_{3}(\mu)\Gamma(\mu),\ \varphi_{2}(\mu)=i\mu^{-3}(2-\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}a_{2}(\mu)\chi_{3}(\mu)\Gamma(\mu).

Observe that
\mu^{\prime}(\tilde{\theta}_{+})
contributes a factor of
(2-\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}
. Consequently, from (
2.14
) we obtain that for
\mu\in[2-\mu_{0},2)
,

\displaystyle\Big\|\frac{d^{k}(\Gamma(\mu(\tilde{\theta}_{+})))}{d\tilde{\theta}_{+}}\Big\|_{\ell^{2}\rightarrow\ell^{2}}\lesssim(2-\mu)^{\frac{2-k}{2}},\quad k=0,1,2.

This estimate together with argument analogous to case (1) gives that
K^{\pm}_{rj}\in\mathbb{B}(\ell^{p}(\mathbb{Z}))
for all
1\leq p\leq\infty
, and so does
K^{0}_{r}
.

(2)
For
K_{\widetilde{P}_{1}}
, from (
5.5
) and the expression (
5.7
) for
K_{B}
with
B=(2-\mu)B^{0}_{21}
, we have

\displaystyle K_{\widetilde{P}_{1}}(n,m)=\frac{1}{16}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{2}(K^{+,j}_{\widetilde{P}_{1}}+K^{-,j}_{\widetilde{P}_{1}})(n,m),

where

\displaystyle K^{\pm,1}_{\widetilde{P}_{1}}(n,m)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{n+m}\int_{0}^{2}\frac{-\chi_{3}(\mu)(2-\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\mu({\rm sin}\tilde{\theta}_{+})^{2}}\sum\limits_{m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\widetilde{\Phi}^{\pm}_{1}(\mu,\widetilde{N}_{1},\widetilde{M}_{2})(\tilde{v}{\widetilde{P}_{1}}\tilde{v})(m_{1},m_{2})d\mu,

\displaystyle K^{\pm,2}_{\widetilde{P}_{1}}(n,m)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{m}\int_{0}^{2}\frac{-ia_{2}(\mu)(2-\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\mu^{2}{\rm sin}\tilde{\theta}_{+}}\chi_{3}(\mu)\sum\limits_{m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\widetilde{\Phi}^{\pm}_{2}(\mu,\widetilde{N}_{1},\widetilde{M}_{2})({v}{\widetilde{P}_{1}}\tilde{v})(m_{1},m_{2})d\mu.

Note that
K^{\pm,2}_{\widetilde{P}_{1}}=O(1)
as
\mu\rightarrow 2
, this means that through a treatment similar to
\widetilde{K}^{\pm,2}_{0}
, one has

K^{\pm,2}_{\widetilde{P}_{1}}(n,m)=O(\left<|n|\pm|m|\right>^{-2}).

As for
K^{\pm,1}_{\widetilde{P}_{1}}
, we first apply the variable substitution (
5.11
), and then do the same decomposition (
3.34
) as
K^{\pm,1}_{P_{1}}
in Proposition
3.8
obtaining

\displaystyle K^{\pm,1}_{\widetilde{P}_{1}}(n,m)=4(-1)^{n+m}\big(k^{+}_{1}(n,m)+k^{-}_{1}(n,m)\big)+O(\left<|n|\pm|m|\right>^{-2}).

Here we also used the fact that
\sum\limits_{m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}(\tilde{v}\widetilde{P}_{1}\tilde{v})(m_{1},m_{2})=-32i.

Thus we have

\displaystyle K_{\widetilde{P}_{1}}(n,m)=\frac{(-1)^{n+m}}{4}g_{1,0}(n,m)+O(\left<|n|\pm|m|\right>^{-2}),

(5.14)

where
g_{1,0}(n,m)
is defined in (
3.50
). Therefore,

K_{\widetilde{P}_{1}}\in\mathbb{B}(\ell^{p}(\mathbb{Z}))
for all
1<p<\infty
by Lemmas
3.4
and
3.7
, and we complete the whole proof.
∎

Remark 5.5
.

Compared with
\mathcal{K}_{1}
discussed in Section
3
, further remarks are given as follows.

(1) We remark that both variable substitution (
3.10
) and (
5.11
) play important roles in estimating the integral kernels. However, they exhibit slight differences in addressing singularity. Specifically, (
3.10
) does not alter the singularity near
\mu=0
, whereas (
5.11
) decreases a singularity of order
(2-\mu)^{-\frac{1}{2}}
.

(2) Moreover, recalling from (
5.2
) that

\displaystyle R^{+}_{0}(\mu^{4})vBv(R^{+}_{0}-R^{-}_{0})(\mu^{4})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4\mu^{4}}\big[JR^{-}_{-\Delta}(4-\mu^{2})\tilde{v}B\tilde{v}(R^{-}_{-\Delta}-R^{+}_{-\Delta})(4-\mu^{2})J

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+R_{-\Delta}(-\mu^{2}){v}B\tilde{v}(R^{-}_{-\Delta}-R^{+}_{-\Delta})(4-\mu^{2})J\big],

(5.15)

we observe that the singularity of the second term is always weaker than that of the first term. This differs from the zero resonance case, where both terms exhibit the same singularity. Due to this difference, the second term demonstrates better boundedness at the endpoints
p=1
and
p=\infty
, simplifying the endpoint analysis compared to the zero resonance case.

(3) We notice that the method used for
K^{\pm,2}_{0}(n,m)
cannot be applied to the integral kernel corresponding to the second term in (
5.5
), since
\tilde{\theta}^{\prime}_{+}(\mu)
becomes singular near
\mu=2
.

5.2.
16
is a resonance of
H

In this subsection, we consider the case where
16
is a resonance of
H
. Taking the expansion

\displaystyle M^{-1}(\mu)

\displaystyle=(2-\mu)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\widetilde{S}_{0}B_{-1}\widetilde{S}_{0}+\big(\widetilde{S}_{0}B^{1}_{01}+B^{1}_{02}\widetilde{S}_{0}+\widetilde{Q}B^{1}_{03}\widetilde{Q}\big)+(2-\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}(\widetilde{Q}B^{1}_{11}+B^{1}_{12}\widetilde{Q})

\displaystyle\quad+(2-\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}\widetilde{P}_{1}+(2-\mu)B^{1}_{21}+\Gamma^{1}_{\frac{3}{2}}(2-\mu)

into (
5.1
), then
\mathcal{K}_{3}
can be written as

\mathcal{K}_{3}=\sum\limits_{B\in\mathcal{B}_{11}\cup\mathcal{B}_{12}}K_{B}+K_{\widetilde{P}_{1}}+K^{1}_{r},

(5.16)

where
K_{B},K_{\widetilde{P}_{1}}
are defined in (
5.5
),
\mathcal{B}_{11}=\{(2-\mu)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\widetilde{S}_{0}B_{-1}\widetilde{S}_{0},\widetilde{S}_{0}B^{1}_{01},B^{1}_{02}\widetilde{S}_{0}\}
and

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{12}

\displaystyle=\{\widetilde{Q}B^{1}_{03}\widetilde{Q},(2-\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}\widetilde{Q}B^{1}_{11},(2-\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}B^{1}_{12}\widetilde{Q},(2-\mu)B^{1}_{21}\},

\displaystyle K^{1}_{r}(n,m)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{2}\mu^{3}\chi_{3}(\mu)\big[R^{+}_{0}(\mu^{4})v\Gamma^{1}_{\frac{3}{2}}(2-\mu)v(R^{+}_{0}-R^{-}_{0})(\mu^{4})\big](n,m)d\mu.

Recalling from the established result in Proposition
5.4
, we have

•

K\in\mathbb{B}(\ell^{p}(\mathbb{Z}))\ {\rm for\ all\ }1\leq p\leq\infty
with
K\in\{K^{1}_{r}\}\cup\{K_{B}:B\in\mathcal{B}_{12}\}
,

•

K_{\widetilde{P}_{1}}\in\mathbb{B}(\ell^{p}(\mathbb{Z}))\ {\rm for\ all\ }1<p<\infty
.

Hence, it suffices to focus on the operators
K_{B}
with
B\in\mathcal{B}_{11}
. Compared to the regular case, these additional terms, while being of the same order
O((2-\mu)^{-\frac{1}{2}})
as
K_{\widetilde{P}_{1}}
in the vicinity of
\mu=2
,
exhibit more subtle behaviors in boundedness analysis which slightly differs in handling. Precisely,

Proposition 5.6
.

Let
H=\Delta^{2}+V
with
|V(n)|\lesssim\left<n\right>^{-\beta}
for
\beta>13
. Suppose that
H
has no positive eigenvalues in the interval
\rm{(}0,16\rm{)}
and
16
is a resonance of
H
. Then for any
B\in\mathcal{B}_{11}
,
K_{B}\in\mathbb{B}(\ell^{p}(\mathbb{Z}))
for all
1<p<\infty
and thus
\mathcal{K}_{3}\in\mathbb{B}(\ell^{p}(\mathbb{Z}))
for any
1<p<\infty
.

Proof.

(1)
For
B=(2-\mu)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\widetilde{S}_{0}B_{-1}\widetilde{S}_{0}
, combining (
5.7
) and the expression of
K_{B}
, we have

\displaystyle\bm{\widetilde{K}_{-1}(n,m)}:

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{2}(2-\mu)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mu^{3}\chi_{3}(\mu)\big[R^{+}_{0}(\mu^{4})v\widetilde{S}_{0}B_{-1}\widetilde{S}_{0}v(R^{+}_{0}-R^{-}_{0})(\mu^{4})\big](n,m)d\mu,

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{16}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{2}(\widetilde{K}^{+,j}_{-1}-\widetilde{K}^{-,j}_{-1})(n,m),

where
N_{1},\widetilde{N}_{1},M_{2}
are defined in (
5.7
) and

\displaystyle\widetilde{K}^{\pm,1}_{-1}(n,m)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{0}^{1}(-1)^{n}({\rm sign}(N_{1}))\int_{0}^{2}\frac{(2-\mu)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\tilde{\theta}^{2}_{+}}{\mu({\rm sin}\tilde{\theta}_{+})^{2}}\chi_{3}(\mu)\sum\limits_{m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{0}^{1}\widetilde{\Phi}^{\pm}_{1}(\mu,N_{1},M_{2})

\displaystyle\quad\times\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{(1)}_{-1}(m,M_{2},m_{1},m_{2})d\rho_{2}d\mu d\rho_{1}:=\sum\limits_{m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{0}^{1}(-1)^{n}({\rm sign}(N_{1}))k^{\pm,1}_{-1}(m_{1},\rho_{1},n,m)d\rho_{1},

\displaystyle\widetilde{K}^{\pm,2}_{-1}(n,m)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{0}^{2}\frac{(2-\mu)^{-\frac{1}{2}}a_{2}(\mu)\tilde{\theta}_{+}}{-\mu^{2}{\rm sin}\tilde{\theta}_{+}}\chi_{3}(\mu)\sum\limits_{m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{0}^{1}\widetilde{\Phi}^{\pm}_{2}(\mu,\widetilde{N}_{1},M_{2})\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{(2)}_{-1}(m,M_{2},m_{1},m_{2})d\rho_{2}d\mu

\displaystyle:=\sum\limits_{m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}}k^{\pm,2}_{-1}(m_{1},n,m),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{(1)}_{-1}(m,

\displaystyle M_{2},m_{1},m_{2})=(-1)^{m}({\rm sign}(M_{2}))(\tilde{v}_{1}\widetilde{S}_{0}B_{-1}\widetilde{S}_{0}\tilde{v}_{1})(m_{1},m_{2}),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{(2)}_{-1}(m,

\displaystyle M_{2},m_{1},m_{2})=(-1)^{m}({\rm sign}(M_{2}))({v}\widetilde{S}_{0}B_{-1}\widetilde{S}_{0}\tilde{v}_{1})(m_{1},m_{2}).

For any fixed
(m_{1},\rho_{1})\in\mathbb{Z}\times[0,1]
, first perform the variable substitution (
5.11
) to
k^{\pm,1}_{-1}(m_{1},\rho_{1},n,m)
into the form (
5.9
) and
k^{\pm,2}_{-1}(m_{1},n,m)
to the form (
5.10
), and then do the similar decomposition (
3.34
), we obtain that

\displaystyle k^{\pm,1}_{-1}(m_{1},\rho_{1},n,m)

\displaystyle=\frac{-i}{2}k^{\pm}_{1}(n,m)\widetilde{C}_{1}(m_{1},m)+O(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{-1}(m_{1})\left<|n|\pm|m|\right>^{-2}),

\displaystyle k^{\pm,2}_{-1}(m_{1},n,m)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\sqrt{2}}{8}q^{|\widetilde{N}_{1}|}k^{\mp}_{2}(n,m)\widetilde{C}_{2}(m_{1},m)+O(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{-1}(m_{1})\left<|n|\pm|m|\right>^{-2}),

where
q=3-2\sqrt{2}
,
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{-1}(m_{1})=\left<m_{1}\right>^{3}|v(m_{1})|\big(|\widetilde{S}_{0}B_{-1}\widetilde{S}_{0}|(\left<\cdot\right>^{3}|v(\cdot)|)\big)(m_{1})
and

\displaystyle\widetilde{C}_{j}(m_{1},m)=\sum\limits_{m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{0}^{1}\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{(j)}_{-1}(m,M_{2},m_{1},m_{2})d\rho_{2}.

Notice that
(-1)^{n}({\rm sign}(N_{1}))
,
q^{|\widetilde{N}_{1}|}
are uniformly bounded in
n,m_{1},\rho_{1}
, and
|\widetilde{C}_{j}(m_{1},m)|\leq\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{-1}(m_{1})
uniformly in
m
for
j=1,2
. It means that
\widetilde{K}_{-1}\in\mathbb{B}(\ell^{p}(\mathbb{Z}))
for all
1<p<\infty
by following the argument as
K_{1}
in Proposition
3.8
.

(2)
For
B=\widetilde{S}_{0}B^{1}_{01}
, it follows from (
5.7
) that

\displaystyle\bm{\widetilde{K}_{01}(n,m)}:=\int_{0}^{2}\mu^{3}\chi_{3}(\mu)\big[R^{+}_{0}(\mu^{4})v\widetilde{S}_{0}B^{1}_{01}v(R^{+}_{0}-R^{-}_{0})(\mu^{4})\big](n,m)d\mu:=\frac{1}{16}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{2}(\widetilde{K}^{+,j}_{01}+\widetilde{K}^{-,j}_{01})(n,m),

where
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{1}_{01}(m_{1},m_{2})=(\tilde{v}_{1}\widetilde{S}_{0}B^{1}_{01}\tilde{v})(m_{1},m_{2})
and

\displaystyle\widetilde{K}^{\pm,1}_{01}(n,m)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{n+m}\int_{0}^{2}\frac{i\tilde{\theta}_{+}\chi_{3}(\mu)}{\mu({\rm sin}\tilde{\theta}_{+})^{2}}\sum\limits_{m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{0}^{1}\widetilde{\Phi}^{\pm}_{1}(\mu,N_{1},\widetilde{M}_{2})({\rm sign}(N_{1}))d\rho_{1}\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{1}_{01}(m_{1},m_{2})d\mu,

\displaystyle\widetilde{K}^{\pm,2}_{01}(n,m)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{m}\sum\limits_{m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}(v\widetilde{S}_{0}B^{1}_{01}\tilde{v})(m_{1},m_{2})\int_{0}^{2}\frac{-ia_{2}(\mu)\chi_{3}(\mu)}{\mu^{2}{\rm sin}\tilde{\theta}_{+}}\widetilde{\Phi}^{\pm}_{2}(\mu,\widetilde{N}_{1},\widetilde{M}_{2})d\mu.

Applying the method for
k^{\pm,1}_{-1}
to
\widetilde{K}^{\pm,1}_{01}
directly and
k^{\pm,2}_{-1}
to the inner integral of
\widetilde{K}^{\pm,2}_{01}
and using the fact that

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{m\in\mathbb{Z},a,b\in\mathbb{R}}\sum\limits_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\left<|n-a|\pm|m-b|\right>^{-2}+\sup\limits_{n\in\mathbb{Z},a,b\in\mathbb{R}}\sum\limits_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\left<|n-a|\pm|m-b|\right>^{-2}<\infty,

(5.17)

we obtain

\displaystyle\widetilde{K}_{01}(n,m)

\displaystyle=-\frac{(-1)^{n+m}}{64}\widetilde{C}^{1}_{01}(n)g_{1,0}(n,m)+\frac{(-1)^{m}\sqrt{2}i}{256}\sum\limits_{m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}(v\widetilde{S}_{0}B^{1}_{01}\tilde{v})(m_{1},m_{2})q^{|\widetilde{N}_{1}|}g_{0,1}(\widetilde{N}_{1},\widetilde{M}_{2})

\displaystyle\quad+R_{01}(n,m),

where the integral operator
R_{01}\in\mathbb{B}(\ell^{p}(\mathbb{Z}))
for all
1\leq p\leq\infty
and

\displaystyle\widetilde{C}^{1}_{01}(n)=\sum\limits_{m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{0}^{1}({\rm sign}(N_{1}))d\rho_{1}\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{1}_{01}(m_{1},m_{2}).

(5.18)

Notice that

\displaystyle q^{|\widetilde{N}_{1}|}g_{0,1}(\widetilde{N}_{1},\widetilde{M}_{2})

\displaystyle={\mathbf{1}}_{E}(\widetilde{N}_{1},\widetilde{M}_{2})\frac{2|\widetilde{N}_{1}|q^{|\widetilde{N}_{1}|}}{(\widetilde{N}_{1})^{2}+(\widetilde{M}_{2})^{2}}+q^{|\widetilde{N}_{1}|}g_{0,1}(\widetilde{N}_{1},\widetilde{M}_{2}){\mathbf{1}}_{E^{c}}(\widetilde{N}_{1},\widetilde{M}_{2})

\displaystyle=O(\big<|\widetilde{N}_{1}|-|\widetilde{M}_{2}|\big>^{-2}),

where
\mathbf{1}_{E}
denotes the characteristic function on the set
E=\{(x,y):||x|-|y||\geq 2\}
and
E^{c}
corresponds to the complementary set. Moreover, in the second equality we used the uniform boundedness of
|\widetilde{N}_{1}|q^{|\widetilde{N}_{1}|}
and
k^{\pm}_{1}(n,m)
. This estimate combined with (
5.17
)
yields that

\displaystyle\widetilde{K}_{01}(n,m)

\displaystyle=-\frac{(-1)^{n+m}}{64}\widetilde{C}^{1}_{01}(n)g_{1,0}(n,m)+\widetilde{{R}}_{01}(n,m),

(5.19)

where the integral operator
\widetilde{{R}}_{01}\in\mathbb{B}(\ell^{p}(\mathbb{Z}))
for all
1\leq p\leq\infty
. Therefore, combining the uniform boundedness of
\widetilde{C}^{1}_{01}(n)
and Lemma
3.7
, we obtain
\widetilde{K}_{01}\in\mathbb{B}(\ell^{p}(\mathbb{Z}))
for all
